Now I have enough context to provide a comprehensive report. Let me create a detailed analysis of the lifecycle and identify the gaps.

## Complete Manual-Commit Strategy Lifecycle Analysis

Based on my investigation, here's the comprehensive flow and where the gaps exist:

### 1. **Hooks System Architecture**

**Four Git Hooks Installed** (`hooks.go`):
- `prepare-commit-msg`: Adds checkpoint trailer to commits
- `commit-msg`: Validates/strips empty commit messages  
- `post-commit`: Condenses session data after user commits
- `pre-push`: Pushes `entire/sessions` branch with user pushes

**Hook Invocation Flow**:
```
User commits → Git hook (e.g., prepare-commit-msg) 
            → `entire hooks git prepare-commit-msg <file> <source>` (hooks_git_cmd.go)
            → strategy.PrepareCommitMsg() (if implements interface)
```

---

### 2. **Session Lifecycle: The Complete Flow**

#### **Phase 1: Session Start (SessionStart hook)**
```
Claude Code fires: SessionStart → handleClaudeCodeSessionStart()
├─ Check for concurrent sessions with checkpoints (warning shown)
├─ Write current session to .entire/current-session.txt
└─ No strategy action yet
```

#### **Phase 2: User Submits Prompt (UserPromptSubmit hook)**
```
Claude fires: UserPromptSubmit → captureInitialState()
├─ Parse hook input (session ID, transcript path)
├─ Capture pre-prompt state:
│   ├─ Untracked files (for new file detection)
│   ├─ Transcript position (last identifier + line count)
│   └─ Store in .entire/state/<session-id>-pre.json
│
└─ strategy.InitializeSession() [manual_commit_hooks.go:786]
    ├─ Load existing session state from .git/entire-sessions/<id>.json
    ├─ If exists && BaseCommit != "":
    │   ├─ Backfill AgentType if empty
    │   ├─ Update TranscriptPath
    │   ├─ Clear LastCheckpointID (starting fresh work)
    │   ├─ Calculate attribution at prompt start (BEFORE agent runs)
    │   │   └─ Store in state.PendingPromptAttribution
    │   ├─ Check if HEAD moved (user committed/pulled):
    │   │   ├─ If old shadow branch exists → migrate it to new HEAD
    │   │   └─ Update state.BaseCommit to new HEAD
    │   └─ Save updated state
    │
    └─ Else: Initialize new session
        ├─ Create SessionState with BaseCommit = HEAD
        ├─ Capture UntrackedFilesAtStart
        ├─ Calculate prompt attribution (for pre-prompt edits)
        └─ Save to .git/entire-sessions/<id>.json
```

**Current State After UserPromptSubmit**:
- Session state file exists in `.git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json`
- Shadow branch does NOT exist yet (created on first SaveChanges)
- `PendingPromptAttribution` calculated and stored

---

#### **Phase 3: Agent Works → User Stops (Stop hook)**
```
Claude fires: Stop → commitWithMetadata()
├─ Load pre-prompt state (untracked files, transcript position)
├─ Copy transcript to .entire/metadata/<session-id>/full.jsonl
├─ Parse transcript (from offset if session state has CondensedTranscriptLines)
├─ Extract prompts, summary, modified files
├─ Compute new/deleted files (git status)
├─ Filter/normalize paths
│
└─ strategy.SaveChanges() [manual_commit_git.go:23]
    ├─ Load session state from .git/entire-sessions/<id>.json
    ├─ Get pending attribution (calculated at prompt start)
    ├─ checkpoint.WriteTemporary():
    │   ├─ Build tree with metadata + modified files
    │   ├─ Create commit on shadow branch (entire/<base>-<worktree>)
    │   └─ Add trailers: Entire-Session, Entire-Metadata, Entire-Strategy
    ├─ Update session state:
    │   ├─ Increment CheckpointCount
    │   ├─ Move PendingPromptAttribution → PromptAttributions[]
    │   ├─ Merge FilesTouched
    │   ├─ Store TranscriptLinesAtStart (first checkpoint only)
    │   └─ Accumulate TokenUsage
    └─ Save session state
```

**Current State After Stop**:
- Shadow branch `entire/<base-commit-hash[:7]>-<worktree-id[:6]>` created
- Session state updated with checkpoint count
- `.entire/metadata/<session-id>/` populated with transcript, prompts, summary

---

#### **Phase 4: User Commits (Manual)**

**4a. prepare-commit-msg Hook Fires**:
```
Git hook: prepare-commit-msg <file> <source>
└─ strategy.PrepareCommitMsg() [manual_commit_hooks.go:188]
    ├─ Skip if git sequence operation (rebase, cherry-pick, revert)
    ├─ Skip if source == "merge", "squash", "commit" (auto-generated)
    │
    ├─ Find sessions for this worktree
    ├─ Filter to sessions with new content:
    │   ├─ Check shadow branch transcript vs CondensedTranscriptLines
    │   └─ OR check live transcript if no shadow branch yet
    │
    ├─ If new content → generate NEW checkpoint ID
    │
    ├─ If no new content BUT LastCheckpointID exists:
    │   ├─ Filter sessions where BaseCommit == current HEAD
    │   ├─ Check if staged files overlap with FilesTouched
    │   └─ Reuse LastCheckpointID if overlap
    │
    ├─ If source == "message" (-m flag):
    │   └─ Ask user via /dev/tty to confirm linking
    │
    └─ Add trailer: "Entire-Checkpoint: <checkpoint-id>"
        └─ With comment explaining user can remove it
```

**Key Logic**:
- **New content** → New checkpoint ID → Add trailer
- **No new content + LastCheckpointID** → Reuse checkpoint ID → Add trailer
- **No new content + no LastCheckpointID** → Skip (no trailer added)

**4b. commit-msg Hook Fires**:
```
Git hook: commit-msg <file>
└─ strategy.CommitMsg() [manual_commit_hooks.go:80]
    ├─ Check if trailer exists
    ├─ If trailer exists BUT message is empty → strip trailer
    │   └─ Git aborts commit due to empty message
    └─ Return (user can manually remove trailer in editor)
```

**4c. post-commit Hook Fires**:
```
Git hook: post-commit
└─ strategy.PostCommit() [manual_commit_hooks.go:401]
    ├─ Get HEAD commit
    ├─ Check for Entire-Checkpoint trailer
    │   └─ If missing → exit (user removed trailer)
    │
    ├─ Find sessions for this worktree
    ├─ Filter sessions with new content
    │
    ├─ For each session with content:
    │   ├─ CondenseSession():
    │   │   ├─ Extract data from shadow branch (transcript, prompts, files)
    │   │   ├─ Calculate attribution (prompt attributions + shadow→head changes)
    │   │   ├─ Generate summary (if enabled)
    │   │   ├─ checkpoint.WriteCommitted():
    │   │   │   └─ Write to entire/sessions at <checkpoint-id[:2]>/<checkpoint-id[2:]>/
    │   │   └─ Return CondenseResult
    │   │
    │   ├─ Update session state:
    │   │   ├─ BaseCommit = new HEAD (session continues from new commit)
    │   │   ├─ CheckpointCount = 0 (reset - shadow branch gets deleted)
    │   │   ├─ CondensedTranscriptLines = total lines
    │   │   ├─ Clear PromptAttributions (used in condensation)
    │   │   ├─ LastCheckpointID = checkpoint ID (for reuse)
    │   │   └─ Save to disk
    │   │
    │   └─ Delete shadow branch (data now on entire/sessions)
    │
    └─ If no content BUT sessions exist:
        └─ Update BaseCommit for all sessions (prevents stale base)
```

**Critical Insight**: PostCommit does two things:
1. **With new content**: Condense → Update session (BaseCommit=new HEAD, reset count) → Delete shadow
2. **No content**: Just update BaseCommit (for checkpoint reuse scenario)

---

#### **Phase 5: Subsequent Prompts (Resume Session)**

The flow repeats from Phase 2:
- **UserPromptSubmit**: Calculate new attribution, check for HEAD changes
- **Stop**: Create checkpoint on shadow branch (from new BaseCommit if user committed)
- **User commits**: Condense again → session continues

---

### 3. **The Gaps & Issues**

#### **❌ GAP 1: No Trailer When User Commits Before First Stop**

**Scenario**:
```
1. User submits prompt (SessionID="abc123")
2. InitializeSession() creates session state (BaseCommit=HEAD, CheckpointCount=0)
3. Agent works, modifies files
4. User commits BEFORE Claude finishes (no Stop hook yet)
5. prepare-commit-msg fires:
   - Finds session for worktree ✓
   - CheckpointCount=0, so no shadow branch exists
   - No LastCheckpointID yet
   - sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript() checks live transcript
   - BUT: Session state has no TranscriptPath stored yet!
   - Falls back to no new content → no trailer added
```

**Root Cause**: `TranscriptPath` is only set in `InitializeSession()` if provided, but the `Stop` hook handler passes `transcriptPath` parameter. The `UserPromptSubmit` hook doesn't have access to transcript path in manual-commit's InitializeSession.

**Fix Needed**: Store `TranscriptPath` in session state during UserPromptSubmit (from hook input).

---

#### **❌ GAP 2: Attribution Not Captured After Mid-Session Commit**

**Scenario**:
```
1. User submits prompt, agent works
2. User commits mid-session (before Stop)
3. PostCommit condenses → updates BaseCommit, clears PromptAttributions
4. User submits NEXT prompt (resume)
5. InitializeSession() calculates attribution BUT:
   - Uses lastCheckpointTree from shadow branch
   - Shadow branch was just deleted in PostCommit
   - Falls back to baseTree comparison
   - Result: Attribution measures (new base → worktree) instead of 
     (last checkpoint → worktree)
```

**Root Cause**: After condensation, shadow branch is deleted. Next prompt's attribution can't reference "last checkpoint tree" because it's gone.

**Potential Fix**: 
- Option A: Store last checkpoint tree hash in session state
- Option B: Don't delete shadow branch after condensation (keep it for attribution)
- Option C: Accept this limitation - attribution after mid-session commit is approximate

---

#### **❌ GAP 3: FilesTouched Not Updated During Mid-Session Commit**

**Scenario**:
```
1. Checkpoint 1: Agent modifies file1.txt, file2.txt
   - FilesTouched = ["file1.txt", "file2.txt"]
   - User commits mid-session
   - PostCommit condenses but doesn't clear FilesTouched
2. Checkpoint 2: Agent modifies file3.txt
   - FilesTouched still has file1, file2 (never cleared)
   - User commits again
   - prepare-commit-msg checks overlap: staged=["file3.txt"], FilesTouched=["file1.txt","file2.txt","file3.txt"]
   - Overlap exists → adds trailer
3. User makes unrelated commit touching file1.txt
   - prepare-commit-msg finds overlap (file1 in FilesTouched)
   - Adds trailer incorrectly
```

**Root Cause**: `FilesTouched` accumulates across checkpoints but is never cleared after condensation.

**Fix Needed**: Clear `FilesTouched` in PostCommit after condensation (similar to `PromptAttributions`).

---

#### **✓ NO GAP: BaseCommit Update**

**This actually works correctly**:
- PostCommit updates `BaseCommit` to new HEAD after condensation
- Next UserPromptSubmit detects BaseCommit mismatch and migrates shadow branch
- OR if no shadow branch (just condensed), creates new one at new base

---

### 4. **Summary of Lifecycle**

```
UserPromptSubmit Hook:
├─ Initialize/update session state
├─ Calculate attribution BEFORE agent works
└─ Store in .git/entire-sessions/<id>.json

Stop Hook:
├─ Extract transcript, files
└─ Create checkpoint on shadow branch (entire/<base>-<worktree>)

User Commits:
├─ prepare-commit-msg: Add trailer (if new content or LastCheckpointID reuse)
├─ commit-msg: Validate message
└─ post-commit: Condense to entire/sessions, update BaseCommit, delete shadow

Next UserPromptSubmit:
├─ Detect BaseCommit change (if user committed)
├─ Migrate shadow branch OR start fresh from new base
└─ Calculate attribution for new checkpoint
```

---

### 5. **Recommendations**

1. **Fix Gap 1**: Store `TranscriptPath` during UserPromptSubmit for mid-session commit detection
2. **Fix Gap 2**: Decide on post-condensation attribution strategy (store tree hash or accept limitation)
3. **Fix Gap 3**: Clear `FilesTouched` after condensation to prevent stale overlap detection
4. **Add Integration Test**: Test scenario where user commits before first Stop

Good, I have a solid understanding of both issues and the current codebase. Let me now synthesize what I've learned and present the brainstorm.

---

## Brainstorm: ENT-221 + ENT-194 — State Tracking for Mid-Session Commits

### The Core Problem

Both issues describe the same fundamental gap: **the manual-commit strategy assumes a linear flow of `UserPromptSubmit → agent work → Stop → user commits`, but agents (especially Claude) can commit mid-conversation via Bash**, breaking this assumption.

**What happens today:**

```
UserPromptSubmit  →  Agent works  →  Agent commits via Bash  →  Agent continues  →  Stop
     ↓                                       ↓                                        ↓
  Session state                    prepare-commit-msg fires           SaveChanges creates
  initialized                      post-commit fires:                 checkpoint on shadow
  (no shadow                       - condenses (empty shadow!)        branch, but BaseCommit
   branch yet)                     - resets CheckpointCount=0          is now wrong (HEAD moved)
                                   - deletes shadow branch
                                   - updates BaseCommit
```

**Result:** The condensed checkpoint has no/incomplete transcript. The post-Stop checkpoint may be orphaned or reference a stale base. The user loses session data.

### ENT-194 specifically

When an agent does sequential commits in a single turn:
1. First commit triggers `post-commit` → condenses whatever exists (maybe nothing on shadow branch yet)
2. Second commit triggers `post-commit` again → condenses again (empty again)
3. `Stop` fires → creates checkpoint, but BaseCommit has shifted twice

### ENT-221 specifically

Asks whether we need cross-hook state tracking — a way for hooks to know "we're in the middle of an agent session, don't condense yet."

---

### Key Design Questions

Before proposing solutions, I want to surface some questions and tradeoffs:

      "question": "When an agent commits mid-session, should we defer condensation until Stop, or condense immediately but handle the continuation gracefully?",
      "header": "Condense timing",
      "options": [
        {
          "label": "Defer until Stop (Recommended)",
          "description": "Mark session as 'active' during agent work. prepare-commit-msg still adds trailer, but post-commit skips condensation. Stop hook does the final condense covering all commits."
        },
        {
          "label": "Condense per commit",
          "description": "Keep current behavior where each commit condenses, but fix it to preserve shadow branch and accumulate transcript data across commits. More complex but gives per-commit checkpoints."
        },
        {
          "label": "Hybrid approach",
          "description": "Condense per commit BUT also keep the shadow branch alive. Each commit gets its own checkpoint, and Stop creates a final one too. Most data preserved but most complex."
        }
      ],
      "multiSelect": false
    },
    {
      "question": "How should we detect 'agent is currently active' so hooks know to defer?",

          "description": "Add an 'active' boolean or 'phase' field to SessionState in .git/entire-sessions/<id>.json. UserPromptSubmit sets active=true, Stop sets active=false. Hooks check this."
        },
        {
          "label": "Lock file",
          "description": "Create a .git/entire-sessions/<id>.lock file during active sessions. Simpler to check but adds file management. Could go stale if agent crashes."
        },
        {
          "label": "PID-based detection",
          "description": "Store the agent PID in session state. Hooks check if PID is still running. Most robust for crash detection but platform-specific."
        }
      ],
      "multiSelect": false
    },

      "question": "Should the checkpoint trailer still be added to agent-created commits, or only to user-initiated commits?",
      "header": "Trailer policy",
      "options": [
        {
          "label": "Add to all commits (Recommended)",
          "description": "Agent commits get the trailer too. On Stop, condensation covers all commits in the session. The trailer links every commit to the same checkpoint."

        {
          "label": "Skip for agent commits",
          "description": "Only add trailer when session is inactive (user commits manually). Simpler but means agent commits have no metadata link."

    57→	FilesTouched []string `json:"files_touched,omitempty"`
    58→
    59→	// LastCheckpointID is the checkpoint ID from last condensation, reused for subsequent commits without new content
    60→	LastCheckpointID id.CheckpointID `json:"last_checkpoint_id,omitempty"`
    61→
    62→	// AgentType identifies the agent that created this session (e.g., "Claude Code", "Gemini CLI", "Cursor")
    63→	AgentType agent.AgentType `json:"agent_type,omitempty"`
    64→
    65→	// Token usage tracking (accumulated across all checkpoints in this session)
    66→	TokenUsage *agent.TokenUsage `json:"token_usage,omitempty"`
    67→
    68→	// Transcript position when session started (for multi-session checkpoints)
    69→	TranscriptLinesAtStart      int    `json:"transcript_lines_at_start,omitempty"`
    70→	TranscriptIdentifierAtStart string `json:"transcript_identifier_at_start,omitempty"`
    71→
    72→	// TranscriptPath is the path to the live transcript file (for mid-session commit detection)
    73→	TranscriptPath string `json:"transcript_path,omitempty"`
    74→
    75→	// PromptAttributions tracks user and agent line changes at each prompt start.
    76→	// This enables accurate attribution by capturing user edits between checkpoints.
    77→	PromptAttributions []PromptAttribution `json:"prompt_attributions,omitempty"`
    78→
    79→	// PendingPromptAttribution holds attribution calculated at prompt start (before agent runs).
    80→	// This is moved to PromptAttributions when SaveChanges is called.
    81→	PendingPromptAttribution *PromptAttribution `json:"pending_prompt_attribution,omitempty"`
    82→}
    83→
    84→// PromptAttribution captures line-level attribution data at the start of each prompt.
    85→// By recording what changed since the last checkpoint BEFORE the agent works,
    86→// we can accurately separate user edits from agent contributions.
    87→type PromptAttribution struct {
    88→	// CheckpointNumber is which checkpoint this was recorded before (1-indexed)
    89→	CheckpointNumber int `json:"checkpoint_number"`
    90→
    91→	// UserLinesAdded is lines added by user since the last checkpoint
    92→	UserLinesAdded int `json:"user_lines_added"`
    93→
    94→	// UserLinesRemoved is lines removed by user since the last checkpoint
    95→	UserLinesRemoved int `json:"user_lines_removed"`
    96→
    97→	// AgentLinesAdded is total agent lines added so far (base → last checkpoint).
    98→	// Always 0 for checkpoint 1 since there's no previous checkpoint to measure against.
    99→	AgentLinesAdded int `json:"agent_lines_added"`
   100→
   101→	// AgentLinesRemoved is total agent lines removed so far (base → last checkpoint).
   102→	// Always 0 for checkpoint 1 since there's no previous checkpoint to measure against.
   103→	AgentLinesRemoved int `json:"agent_lines_removed"`
   104→
   105→	// UserAddedPerFile tracks per-file user additions for accurate modification tracking.
   106→	// This enables distinguishing user self-modifications from agent modifications.
   107→	// See docs/architecture/attribution.md for details.
   108→	UserAddedPerFile map[string]int `json:"user_added_per_file,omitempty"`
   109→}
   110→
   111→// StateStore provides low-level operations for managing session state files.
   112→//
   113→// StateStore is a primitive for session state persistence. It is NOT the same as
   114→// the Sessions interface - it only handles state files in .git/entire-sessions/,
   115→// not the full session data which includes checkpoint content.
   116→//
   117→// Use StateStore directly in strategies for performance-critical state operations.
   118→// Use the Sessions interface (when implemented) for high-level session management.
   119→type StateStore struct {
   120→	// stateDir is the directory where session state files are stored
   121→	stateDir string
   122→}
   123→
   124→// NewStateStore creates a new state store.
   125→// Uses the git common dir to store session state (shared across worktrees).
   126→func NewStateStore() (*StateStore, error) {
   127→	commonDir, err := getGitCommonDir()
   128→	if err != nil {
   129→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get git common dir: %w", err)
   130→	}
   131→	return &StateStore{
   132→		stateDir: filepath.Join(commonDir, sessionStateDirName),
   133→	}, nil
   134→}
   135→
   136→// NewStateStoreWithDir creates a new state store with a custom directory.
   137→// This is useful for testing.
   138→func NewStateStoreWithDir(stateDir string) *StateStore {
   139→	return &StateStore{stateDir: stateDir}
   140→}
   141→
   142→// Load loads the session state for the given session ID.
   143→// Returns (nil, nil) when session file doesn't exist (not an error condition).
   144→func (s *StateStore) Load(ctx context.Context, sessionID string) (*State, error) {
   145→	_ = ctx // Reserved for future use
   146→
   147→	// Validate session ID to prevent path traversal
   148→	if err := validation.ValidateSessionID(sessionID); err != nil {
   149→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("invalid session ID: %w", err)
   150→	}
   151→
   152→	stateFile := s.stateFilePath(sessionID)
   153→
   154→	data, err := os.ReadFile(stateFile) //nolint:gosec // stateFile is derived from sessionID
   155→	if os.IsNotExist(err) {
   156→		return nil, nil //nolint:nilnil // nil,nil indicates session not found (expected case)
   157→	}
   158→	if err != nil {
   159→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read session state: %w", err)
   160→	}
   161→
   162→	var state State
   163→	if err := json.Unmarshal(data, &state); err != nil {
   164→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to unmarshal session state: %w", err)
   165→	}
   166→	return &state, nil
   167→}
   168→
   169→// Save saves the session state atomically.
   170→func (s *StateStore) Save(ctx context.Context, state *State) error {
   171→	_ = ctx // Reserved for future use
   172→
   173→	// Validate session ID to prevent path traversal
   174→	if err := validation.ValidateSessionID(state.SessionID); err != nil {
   175→		return fmt.Errorf("invalid session ID: %w", err)
   176→	}
   177→
   178→	if err := os.MkdirAll(s.stateDir, 0o750); err != nil {
   179→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to create session state directory: %w", err)
   180→	}
   181→
   182→	data, err := jsonutil.MarshalIndentWithNewline(state, "", "  ")
   183→	if err != nil {
   184→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal session state: %w", err)
   185→	}
   186→
   187→	stateFile := s.stateFilePath(state.SessionID)
   188→
   189→	// Atomic write: write to temp file, then rename
   190→	tmpFile := stateFile + ".tmp"
   191→	if err := os.WriteFile(tmpFile, data, 0o600); err != nil {
   192→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to write session state: %w", err)
   193→	}
   194→	if err := os.Rename(tmpFile, stateFile); err != nil {
   195→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to rename session state file: %w", err)
   196→	}
   197→	return nil
   198→}
   199→
   200→// Clear removes the session state file for the given session ID.
   201→func (s *StateStore) Clear(ctx context.Context, sessionID string) error {
   202→	_ = ctx // Reserved for future use
   203→
   204→	// Validate session ID to prevent path traversal
   205→	if err := validation.ValidateSessionID(sessionID); err != nil {
   206→		return fmt.Errorf("invalid session ID: %w", err)
   207→	}
   208→
   209→	stateFile := s.stateFilePath(sessionID)
   210→
   211→	if err := os.Remove(stateFile); err != nil {
   212→		if os.IsNotExist(err) {
   213→			return nil // Already gone, not an error
   214→		}
   215→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to remove session state file: %w", err)
   216→	}
   217→	return nil
   218→}
   219→
   220→// RemoveAll removes the entire session state directory.
   221→// This is used during uninstall to completely remove all session state.
   222→func (s *StateStore) RemoveAll() error {
   223→	if err := os.RemoveAll(s.stateDir); err != nil {
   224→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to remove session state directory: %w", err)
   225→	}
   226→	return nil
   227→}
   228→
   229→// List returns all session states.
   230→func (s *StateStore) List(ctx context.Context) ([]*State, error) {
   231→	_ = ctx // Reserved for future use
   232→
   233→	entries, err := os.ReadDir(s.stateDir)
   234→	if os.IsNotExist(err) {
   235→		return nil, nil
   236→	}
   237→	if err != nil {
   238→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to read session state directory: %w", err)
   239→	}
   240→
   241→	var states []*State
   242→	for _, entry := range entries {
   243→		if entry.IsDir() || !strings.HasSuffix(entry.Name(), ".json") {
   244→			continue
   245→		}
   246→		if strings.HasSuffix(entry.Name(), ".tmp") {
   247→			continue // Skip temp files
   248→		}
   249→
   250→		sessionID := strings.TrimSuffix(entry.Name(), ".json")
   251→		state, err := s.Load(ctx, sessionID)
   252→		if err != nil {
   253→			continue // Skip corrupted state files
   254→		}
   255→		if state == nil {
   256→			continue
   257→		}
   258→
   259→		states = append(states, state)
   260→	}
   261→	return states, nil
   262→}
   263→
   264→// stateFilePath returns the path to a session state file.
   265→func (s *StateStore) stateFilePath(sessionID string) string {
   266→	return filepath.Join(s.stateDir, sessionID+".json")
   267→}
   268→
   269→// getGitCommonDir returns the path to the shared git directory.
   270→// In a regular checkout, this is .git/
   271→// In a worktree, this is the main repo's .git/ (not .git/worktrees/<name>/)
   272→func getGitCommonDir() (string, error) {
   273→	ctx := context.Background()
   274→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "rev-parse", "--git-common-dir")
   275→	cmd.Dir = "."
   276→	output, err := cmd.Output()
   277→	if err != nil {
   278→		return "", fmt.Errorf("failed to get git common dir: %w", err)
   279→	}
   280→
   281→	commonDir := strings.TrimSpace(string(output))
   282→
   283→	// git rev-parse --git-common-dir returns relative paths from the working directory,
   284→	// so we need to make it absolute if it isn't already
   285→	if !filepath.IsAbs(commonDir) {
   286→		commonDir = filepath.Join(".", commonDir)
   287→	}
   288→
   289→	return filepath.Clean(commonDir), nil
   290→}
   291→

But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.

> AGENT

Yes — this is the right thing to worry about. Let me break down the concurrent access situation.

### Current state: atomic writes, but no locking

`Save()` at `state.go:170` does an atomic write (write to `.tmp`, rename), which prevents **partial reads**. But there's no protection against **read-modify-write races** — two processes could both `Load()`, modify independently, and `Save()`, with the second overwriting the first's changes.

### Does this race actually happen today?

For a **single session**, the hooks are effectively sequential:

```
Agent calls `git commit` via Bash (blocking)
  → prepare-commit-msg  (reads/writes state)
  → commit-msg           (reads state)
  → git writes commit object
  → post-commit          (reads/writes state)
  → git commit returns
Agent continues working
  → Stop hook fires      (reads/writes state)
```

Each hook is a separate process, but git runs them sequentially, and the Bash call blocks until `git commit` returns. So no overlap within a single session.

For **concurrent sessions** (two agents, same directory), they have different session IDs → different state files → no conflict on writes. But `PostCommit()` calls `ListForWorktree()` which scans all state files — it could see a stale snapshot of another session's state. Low impact since it's just deciding whether to condense.

### The real problem for the proposed "active" flag

The race condition isn't the concern. The concern is **stale state on crash**:

1. `UserPromptSubmit` sets `active=true`
2. Agent crashes / user kills terminal / network drops
3. `Stop` never fires → `active=true` forever
4. Next time user commits manually → `post-commit` sees `active=true`, defers condensation indefinitely

This means any detection mechanism needs a **staleness check**.

> AGENT

Great question. Let me think through the checkpoint ID lifecycle across hooks in the deferred model.

### The challenge

Today checkpoint IDs are ephemeral — generated in `prepare-commit-msg`, consumed in `post-commit`, done. With deferred condensation, we need the ID to survive across multiple commits and the eventual condensation.

### Proposed flow

```
Agent commit #1:
  prepare-commit-msg → generates checkpoint ID "abc123", 
                        stores in state.PendingCheckpointID,
                        adds trailer to commit

  post-commit → sees session is active → SKIPS condensation
                (shadow branch + state preserved)

Agent commit #2:
  prepare-commit-msg → reads state.PendingCheckpointID ("abc123"),
                        REUSES it, adds same trailer

  post-commit → still active → skips again

Stop hook fires:
  SaveChanges → writes final checkpoint to shadow branch
                 (full transcript, all files from entire session)

User commits manually:
  prepare-commit-msg → session inactive, has PendingCheckpointID → 
                        reuses "abc123", adds trailer

  post-commit → session inactive → condenses from shadow branch
                to entire/sessions at shard abc123
                Clears PendingCheckpointID, sets LastCheckpointID
```

**Key: one checkpoint ID per session-commit-cycle.** All agent commits + the user's final commit share the same ID, pointing to one metadata entry that covers the entire session.

### What lives in session state

The new/modified fields would be:

```go
type State struct {
    // ... existing fields ...
    
    // PendingCheckpointID is set by prepare-commit-msg on the first 
    // commit in an active session. Reused for all subsequent commits
    // until condensation clears it.
    PendingCheckpointID id.CheckpointID `json:"pending_checkpoint_id,omitempty"`
    
    // We can use LastInteractionTime (from PR #165) + EndedAt 
    // to determine if a session is active, avoiding a separate flag.
}
```

And the "is session active?" check in `post-commit` would be:

```go
func isSessionActive(state *State) bool {
    if state.EndedAt != nil {
        return false  // explicitly ended
    }
    // Use lastInteractionTime from PR #165 as liveness signal
    if time.Since(state.LastInteractionTime) > 30*time.Minute {
        return false  // stale, treat as inactive
    }
    return true
}
```

### One concern with this model

If the user **never commits** after the session ends (e.g., they `git stash` or discard), the checkpoint ID is burned — it's in the agent's commits but never condensed. The shadow branch still has the data though, so it's not lost — just not on `entire/sessions` yet.

Is that acceptable, or does condensation need to happen at `Stop` regardless of whether a user commit follows?

> AGENT

Good point — let me address that directly.

### The actual concurrent access risk

The concern isn't two hooks for the **same** session racing (those are sequential within a git commit), it's:

**Scenario A: User commits in another terminal while agent is mid-session**
```
Terminal 1 (agent):                    Terminal 2 (user):
  UserPromptSubmit → writes state
  Agent working...
    Agent calls `git commit`
      prepare-commit-msg (proc A)       User runs `git commit`
        reads state                       prepare-commit-msg (proc B)
        generates PendingCheckpointID       reads state (SAME version)
        writes state                        generates DIFFERENT ID
      post-commit (proc A)                  writes state (OVERWRITES A's ID!)
        reads state                       post-commit (proc B)
        updates BaseCommit                  reads state
        writes state                        updates BaseCommit
                                            writes state (OVERWRITES again!)
```

Proc A and proc B are independent OS processes — git doesn't serialize commits across terminals. Both do read-modify-write on the same state file, and the atomic rename only prevents partial reads, not lost updates.

**Scenario B: Agent does rapid sequential commits** — not a problem, git hooks within one `git commit` are sequential, and Bash blocks until the commit completes.

### Impact in the proposed model

The lost update could cause:
- **PendingCheckpointID overwritten** — one commit's trailer points to a checkpoint ID that doesn't match what's in state
- **BaseCommit overwritten** — shadow branch naming could be wrong
- **CheckpointCount wrong** — if both incremented from the same base value

### Mitigation: file locking

The simplest robust fix is `flock` around read-modify-write cycles:

```go
func (s *StateStore) WithLock(sessionID string, fn func() error) error {
    lockFile := s.stateFilePath(sessionID) + ".lock"
    f, err := os.Create(lockFile)
    if err != nil {
        return err
    }
    defer f.Close()
    defer os.Remove(lockFile)
    
    if err := syscall.Flock(int(f.Fd()), syscall.LOCK_EX); err != nil {
        return err
    }
    defer syscall.Flock(int(f.Fd()), syscall.LOCK_UN)
    
    return fn()
}
```

Each hook does:
```go
store.WithLock(sessionID, func() error {
    state, _ := store.Load(ctx, sessionID)
    // modify state
    return store.Save(ctx, state)
})
```

This is cross-platform on macOS/Linux (where this CLI runs), adds minimal overhead, and fully prevents lost updates.

> AGENT

Good. Let me now consolidate everything into a complete design summary.

---

## Design Summary: Cross-Hook State Tracking for Mid-Session Commits

### Problem Statement
When an agent commits via Bash mid-session, `post-commit` condenses prematurely (often with incomplete/no data), resets state, and deletes the shadow branch. Subsequent checkpoints in the same session are lost or broken.

### Design Decisions

| Decision | Choice |
|----------|--------|
| Condense timing | Defer — skip condensation for active sessions |
| Condense trigger | On user commit (current model), after session is inactive |
| Active detection | `EndedAt` + `LastInteractionTime` (PR #165) with TTL |
| Checkpoint ID | Single `PendingCheckpointID` shared across all commits in a session |
| Mid-session post-commit | Update `BaseCommit` only, skip condensation + shadow branch deletion |
| Scope | Manual-commit strategy only |
| Concurrency | Add `flock`-based file locking to StateStore |

### New/Modified Session State Fields

```go
type State struct {
    // ... existing ...
    
    // PendingCheckpointID is generated on the first commit in an active
    // session and reused for all subsequent commits until condensation.
    PendingCheckpointID id.CheckpointID `json:"pending_checkpoint_id,omitempty"`
}
```

### Hook Behavior Changes

**`prepare-commit-msg`** (with locking):
1. Check if session is active (EndedAt==nil && LastInteractionTime recent)
2. If active + no `PendingCheckpointID` → generate one, save to state
3. If active + has `PendingCheckpointID` → reuse it
4. Add trailer with checkpoint ID (same as today)

**`post-commit`** (with locking):
1. Check if session is active
2. **If active** → update `BaseCommit` to new HEAD, migrate shadow branch. **Skip** condensation, **skip** shadow branch deletion, **skip** resetting CheckpointCount
3. **If inactive** → normal flow (condense, reset, delete shadow branch)

**`SaveChanges` (Stop hook)**:
- No change — writes checkpoint to shadow branch as today
- Shadow branch now has the full session's data (never prematurely deleted)

### Shadow Branch Migration on Mid-Session Commit

When post-commit updates `BaseCommit` for an active session, it must also rename the shadow branch:
```
entire/<old-HEAD[:7]>-<worktreeHash> → entire/<new-HEAD[:7]>-<worktreeHash>
```
This is the same migration logic already in `UserPromptSubmit` (for pull/rebase). We should extract it to a shared helper.

### Concurrency: StateStore Locking

```go
// LoadModifySave atomically reads, modifies, and writes a session state.
func (s *StateStore) LoadModifySave(ctx context.Context, sessionID string, 
    fn func(*State) error) error {
    // flock on <session-id>.json.lock
    // Load → fn(state) → Save
    // unlock
}
```

All hook code paths that do read-modify-write use this instead of separate Load/Save calls.

### One open concern

After an agent's mid-session commit, the shadow branch gets migrated to the new BaseCommit. But the shadow branch's existing commits reference the old base tree. Does `SaveChanges` (which appends to the shadow branch) handle this correctly, or does it assume the shadow branch root matches BaseCommit?

> AGENT

Good call. Let me work through the full state machine, including multi-session.

### Session Phase States

```
┌─────────────┐
│   (none)    │  No state file exists
└──────┬──────┘
       │ SessionStart
       ▼
┌─────────────┐
│    IDLE     │  State file exists, agent not actively working
└──┬───┬───┬──┘
   │   │   │
   │   │   │ UserPromptSubmit
   │   │   ▼
   │   │ ┌─────────────┐
   │   │ │   ACTIVE    │  Agent is working (between UserPromptSubmit and Stop)
   │   │ └──┬───┬───┬──┘
   │   │    │   │   │
   │   │    │   │   │ git commit (agent-initiated)
   │   │    │   │   └──► ACTIVE (self-loop: update BaseCommit, migrate shadow, skip condense)
   │   │    │   │
   │   │    │   │ Stop (SaveChanges)
   │   │    │   └──► IDLE (checkpoint written to shadow branch)
   │   │    │
   │   │    │ SessionStop (agent crashes / force-quit while active)
   │   │    └──► ENDED
   │   │
   │   │ git commit (user-initiated, between turns)
   │   └──► IDLE (condense from shadow → entire/sessions, update BaseCommit)
   │
   │ SessionStop
   ▼
┌─────────────┐
│   ENDED     │  Session fully closed
└──────┬──────┘
       │ git commit (user commits after session ended)
       └──► ENDED (condense any remaining data, clean up)
```

### Transition Table

| Current State | Event | Action | Next State |
|---|---|---|---|
| (none) | `SessionStart` | Create state file | **IDLE** |
| IDLE | `UserPromptSubmit` | Set phase=ACTIVE, calculate attribution | **ACTIVE** |
| ACTIVE | `Stop` | SaveChanges to shadow branch | **IDLE** |
| ACTIVE | `git commit` | Update BaseCommit, migrate shadow branch, add trailer with PendingCheckpointID. **Skip condensation.** | **ACTIVE** |
| ACTIVE | `SessionStop` | Set EndedAt (abnormal — agent quit mid-work) | **ENDED** |
| IDLE | `git commit` | Condense from shadow → entire/sessions, delete shadow branch (if no other sessions need it), reset CheckpointCount | **IDLE** |
| IDLE | `UserPromptSubmit` | Set phase=ACTIVE, calculate attribution | **ACTIVE** |
| IDLE | `SessionStop` | Set EndedAt | **ENDED** |
| ENDED | `git commit` | Condense any remaining data, clean up state | **ENDED** |

### Multi-Session Interactions

Two sessions (A, B) in the same worktree, same BaseCommit → share shadow branch:

```
Time ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────►

Session A:  IDLE ──► ACTIVE ─────────────────────► IDLE ────► ...
Session B:  IDLE ──────────► ACTIVE ──► IDLE ──────────────► ...
                                  │
                                  │ Agent B commits via Bash
                                  ▼
                    post-commit fires, checks ALL sessions:
                      Session A: ACTIVE → skip condense, update BaseCommit
                      Session B: ACTIVE → skip condense, update BaseCommit
                      Shadow branch: migrate (shared by both)
```

**Key multi-session rules for `post-commit`:**

1. **Iterate all sessions for this worktree** (already does this)
2. For each session, check phase independently
3. **If ANY session is ACTIVE** → don't delete shadow branch after condensing others
4. **Only delete shadow branch when ALL sessions on it are condensed or ended**

```
Scenario: B finishes first, user commits

Session A: ACTIVE (still working)
Session B: IDLE (has uncondensed data on shadow)

User commits:
  post-commit:
    Session A: ACTIVE → skip condense, update BaseCommit
    Session B: IDLE → condense B's data to entire/sessions
    Shadow branch: DO NOT DELETE (A still needs it)
    Migrate shadow to new HEAD

Later, A finishes (Stop), user commits again:
  post-commit:
    Session A: IDLE → condense A's data
    No other sessions on shadow → DELETE shadow branch
```

### How to track which sessions have been condensed

We need a way to know if a session's data has been written to `entire/sessions` already, so post-commit doesn't condense it twice and knows when the shadow branch can be deleted.

Options:
- **Existing `CondensedTranscriptLines`** — if > 0, this session has been partially/fully condensed. But it doesn't distinguish "condensed from this shadow branch epoch" vs "condensed in a previous commit cycle."
- **New field `CondensedForCommit`** — stores the commit hash that triggered condensation. If it matches a commit reachable from HEAD, this session's data is already condensed.

Or simpler: the existing `CheckpointCount` reset to 0 on condensation already signals "nothing to condense." We just need to not delete the shadow branch if other sessions still have `CheckpointCount > 0`.

Good catches. Let me revise the state machine based on all your feedback.

### Revised State Machine

**Terminology note:** I'm using "step" for ephemeral shadow branch checkpoints (what `CheckpointCount` tracks) and "checkpoint" for the permanent data on `entire/checkpoints/v1`.

```
                          SessionStart (no state file created)
                                │
                                ▼
                    ┌──────────────────────┐
                    │    UNINITIALIZED     │  SessionStart fired, no state file yet
                    └──────────┬───────────┘
                               │ UserPromptSubmit (creates state file)
                               ▼
              ┌───────────────────────────────────┐
         ┌───►│             ACTIVE                │◄──────────────────────┐
         │    │  Agent working, steps accumulating │                      │
         │    └───┬────────┬──────────┬───────────┘                      │
         │        │        │          │                                   │
         │        │        │          │ git commit (agent mid-session)    │
         │        │        │          ▼                                   │
         │        │        │  ┌───────────────────────────────┐          │
         │        │        │  │     ACTIVE_COMMITTED          │          │
         │        │        │  │  Agent working + has commits  │──┐       │
         │        │        │  │  not yet condensed            │  │       │
         │        │        │  └───────────────┬───────────────┘  │       │
         │        │        │                  │                  │       │
         │        │        │                  │ more git commits │       │
         │        │        │                  └──────────────────┘       │
         │        │        │                  │                          │
         │        │        │ Stop             │ Stop                     │
         │        │        ▼                  ▼                          │
         │    ┌──────────────────────────────────────┐                   │
         │    │              IDLE                     │                   │
         │    │  Shadow branch has steps,             │                   │
         │    │  waiting for user commit to condense  │                   │
         │    └──┬──────────┬───────────┬────────────┘                   │
         │       │          │           │                                │
         │       │          │           │ UserPromptSubmit               │
         │       │          │           └────────────────────────────────┘
         │       │          │
         │       │          │ git commit (user-initiated)
         │       │          ▼
         │       │  Condense shadow → entire/checkpoints/v1
         │       │  Delete shadow (if no other sessions need it)
         │       │  Reset step count
         │       │          │
         │       │          └──► IDLE (with updated BaseCommit)
         │       │
         │       │ SessionStop
         │       ▼
         │  ┌─────────────────────────┐
         │  │         ENDED           │  Clean shutdown, may have uncondensed data
         │  └──┬──────────────────┬───┘
         │     │                  │
         │     │ claude -r        │ git commit
         │     │ (resume)         │ (condense remaining data)
         │     │                  └──► ENDED
         │     │
         │     │ SessionStart + UserPromptSubmit
         │     └──► clear EndedAt
         └─────────────────────────────┘

                    ┌─────────────────────────┐
                    │       TIMED_OUT         │  Detected via stale LastInteractionTime
                    │  (virtual, not stored)   │  Treated as ENDED by hooks
                    └─────────────────────────┘
```

### Key refinements from your feedback

1. **SessionStart creates no state** — UNINITIALIZED is the real starting state. State file only created on first `UserPromptSubmit`.

2. **ACTIVE_COMMITTED** — explicit intermediate state where agent has committed but data isn't on `entire/checkpoints/v1` yet. Useful for `entire status` ("2 commits pending condensation").

3. **ENDED is not terminal** — `claude -r` fires SessionStart + UserPromptSubmit, which clears `EndedAt` and transitions back to ACTIVE. (Currently `InitializeSession` doesn't clear `EndedAt` — that's a bug we'd fix.)

4. **TIMED_OUT is virtual** — not a stored phase. Hooks compute it from `LastInteractionTime > TTL`. Behaviorally equivalent to ENDED (condensation allowed, shadow branch cleanup allowed).

5. **IDLE always has uncondensed data** — if Stop fired, there's at least one step on the shadow branch. The distinction between "idle with data" and "idle without data" is just `CheckpointCount > 0` (or whatever we rename it to).

### How post-commit decides what to do

```
post-commit:
  for each session on this worktree:
    if session.phase == ACTIVE or ACTIVE_COMMITTED:
      → update BaseCommit
      → migrate shadow branch
      → store PendingCheckpointID (if not set)
      → SKIP condensation
    
    if session.phase == IDLE or ENDED or TIMED_OUT:
      → condense to entire/checkpoints/v1
      → reset step count
      → set LastCheckpointID
  
  if ALL sessions condensed (no ACTIVE sessions remain):
    → delete shadow branch
  else:
    → keep shadow branch (active sessions still need it)
```

Does this capture the model you had in mind? Specifically:
- Is ACTIVE_COMMITTED worth tracking as a separate phase, or is "ACTIVE + PendingCheckpointID is set" sufficient?
- Should TIMED_OUT be a stored value or stay virtual?

IDLE ──SessionStop──► ENDED

Perfect! Now I have a complete understanding. Let me create a comprehensive report:

## Manual-Commit Strategy: PostCommit Flow and Condensation Analysis

### 1. **PostCommit() Method Flow** (`manual_commit_hooks.go:401-551`)

The PostCommit hook is called after a user commits and performs these steps:

1. **Initial Checks**:
   - Opens git repository
   - Gets HEAD commit
   - Checks if commit has `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer via `trailers.ParseCheckpoint(commit.Message)`
   - If no trailer found, exits silently (user removed it, treat as manual commit)

2. **Find Sessions for Worktree**:
   - Calls `findSessionsForWorktree(worktreePath)` to find all active sessions in this worktree
   - If no sessions found, logs warning and exits

3. **Filter Sessions with New Content**:
   - Calls `filterSessionsWithNewContent(repo, sessions)` to identify which sessions have new content
   - If no sessions have new content:
     - Updates `BaseCommit` for all sessions to new HEAD (prevents stale BaseCommit)
     - Saves each session state
     - Exits without condensation

4. **Condense Each Session with Content**:
   - For each session with content:
     - Computes shadow branch name BEFORE condensation: `getShadowBranchNameForCommit(state.BaseCommit, state.WorktreeID)`
     - Calls `CondenseSession(repo, checkpointID, state)`
     - Tracks shadow branch name for cleanup
     - Updates session state:
       - `BaseCommit = head.Hash().String()` (new HEAD)
       - `CheckpointCount = 0` (reset, shadow branch will be deleted)
       - `CondensedTranscriptLines = result.TotalTranscriptLines` (track progress)
       - `PromptAttributions = nil` (clear, already used)
       - `PendingPromptAttribution = nil` (clear)
       - `LastCheckpointID = checkpointID` (for reuse on subsequent commits)
     - Saves updated session state
     - Prints condensation message to stderr

5. **Cleanup Shadow Branches**:
   - For each shadow branch tracked during condensation:
     - Calls `deleteShadowBranch(repo, shadowBranchName)`
     - Prints cleanup message to stderr
     - Logs branch deletion

### 2. **CondenseSession() Method** (`manual_commit_condensation.go:106-189`)

The condensation process:

1. **Get Shadow Branch**:
   - Gets shadow branch ref: `entire/<baseCommit[:7]>-<worktreeID[:6]>`
   - If branch not found, returns error

2. **Extract Session Data**:
   - Calls `extractSessionData(repo, ref.Hash(), sessionID, state.FilesTouched, state.AgentType)`
   - Extracts from shadow branch tree:
     - Full transcript (JSONL or JSON depending on agent)
     - User prompts (extracted from transcript)
     - Generated context (from prompts)
     - Files touched (from session state, not tree)
     - Token usage (calculated from transcript)

3. **Get Metadata**:
   - Gets author info: `GetGitAuthorFromRepo(repo)`
   - Calculates attribution: `calculateSessionAttributions(repo, ref, sessionData, state)`
   - Gets current branch name: `GetCurrentBranchName(repo)`

4. **Generate Summary** (if enabled):
   - Scopes transcript to this checkpoint's portion: `transcript.SliceFromLine(sessionData.Transcript, state.TranscriptLinesAtStart)`
   - Calls `summarize.GenerateFromTranscript()` (non-blocking, continues on error)

5. **Write to entire/sessions Branch**:
   - Calls `store.WriteCommitted()` with:
     - CheckpointID (from trailer)
     - SessionID
     - Strategy name ("manual-commit")
     - Branch name
     - Full transcript
     - Prompts and context
     - Files touched
     - Checkpoints count
     - Shadow branch name (ephemeral)
     - Author info
     - Agent type
     - Transcript position markers
     - Token usage
     - Attribution data
     - Summary (if generated)

6. **Return Result**:
   - Returns `CondenseResult` with checkpoint ID, session ID, checkpoints count, files touched, and total transcript lines

### 3. **Shadow Branch Deletion** (`deleteShadowBranch` helper)

Called from PostCommit after successful condensation:

```go
// Deletes the shadow branch reference from git
repo.Storer.RemoveReference(refName)
```

This is safe because:
- Data is already copied to `entire/sessions` branch
- Session state is updated with new BaseCommit and CheckpointCount=0
- Sessions without shadow branches but with LastCheckpointID are kept for checkpoint reuse

### 4. **sessionHasNewContent Checks** (`manual_commit_hooks.go:555-689`)

Two levels of content detection:

#### **sessionHasNewContent()** (`manual_commit_hooks.go:575-613`)
- Gets shadow branch ref
- If shadow branch doesn't exist, falls back to `sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript()`
- If shadow branch exists:
  - Extracts transcript from shadow branch tree
  - Counts transcript lines (trimming trailing empty lines)
  - Returns `transcriptLines > state.CondensedTranscriptLines`

#### **sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript()** (`manual_commit_hooks.go:640-689`)
Used when no shadow branch exists (agent may commit mid-session before Stop):

1. Checks prerequisites: `state.TranscriptPath` and `state.AgentType` must be set
2. Gets agent and casts to `TranscriptAnalyzer`
3. Gets current transcript position: `analyzer.GetTranscriptPosition(state.TranscriptPath)`
4. Checks if transcript grew: `currentPos > state.CondensedTranscriptLines`
5. Extracts modified files from new portion: `analyzer.ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset(state.TranscriptPath, state.CondensedTranscriptLines)`
6. Checks for file overlap with staged files: `hasOverlappingFiles(stagedFiles, modifiedFiles)`
7. Returns `true` only if transcript grew, has file modifications, AND files overlap with staged files

### 5. **PrepareCommitMsg() and Checkpoint ID Generation** (`manual_commit_hooks.go:188-392`)

The checkpoint ID generation flow:

1. **Skip Conditions**:
   - During git sequence operations (rebase, cherry-pick, revert)
   - For merge, squash, or commit (amend) sources

2. **Find Active Sessions**:
   - Finds sessions for current worktree: `findSessionsForWorktree(worktreePath)`
   - Exits if no sessions found

3. **Filter Sessions with Content**:
   - Calls `filterSessionsWithNewContent(repo, sessions)`

4. **Determine Checkpoint ID**:
   - **If sessions have new content**:
     - Sets `hasNewContent = true`
     - Will generate new checkpoint ID below
   - **If no new content**:
     - Gets current HEAD
     - Filters sessions to those matching current HEAD's BaseCommit
     - For each matching session:
       - If `LastCheckpointID` exists AND (`FilesTouched` is empty OR files overlap with staged files):
         - Reuses `LastCheckpointID`
         - Breaks loop
     - If no reusable checkpoint ID found, exits (no trailer added)

5. **Check for Existing Trailer**:
   - Reads commit message file
   - If trailer already exists (e.g., amend), keeps it and exits

6. **Generate or Reuse Checkpoint ID**:
   - **If hasNewContent**: Generate new ID via `id.Generate()` (12-hex-char random)
   - **Otherwise**: Use reused `LastCheckpointID` from step 4

7. **Get Agent Info and Last Prompt**:
   - Determines agent type (from session or default)
   - Gets last prompt via `getLastPrompt(repo, state)`
   - Truncates/collapses prompt for display

8. **Add Trailer**:
   - **If source == "message"** (-m or -F flag):
     - Prompts user via TTY: `askConfirmTTY("Link this commit...")`
     - If user declines, exits
     - Adds trailer: `addCheckpointTrailer(message, checkpointID)`
   - **Otherwise** (editor flow):
     - Adds trailer with comment: `addCheckpointTrailerWithComment(...)`
     - Comment explains user can remove trailer

9. **Write Updated Message**:
   - Writes message back to commit message file

### 6. **migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded()** (`manual_commit_migration.go:21-78`)

Handles HEAD changes mid-session (pull/rebase):

1. **Check if Migration Needed**:
   - Gets current HEAD
   - If `state.BaseCommit == currentHead`, no migration needed (returns false)

2. **Compute Shadow Branch Names**:
   - Old: `checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(state.BaseCommit, state.WorktreeID)`
   - New: `checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(currentHead, state.WorktreeID)`
   - If names are same (hash prefix collision), just updates `state.BaseCommit` and returns

3. **Check if Old Branch Exists**:
   - If old branch doesn't exist:
     - Just updates `state.BaseCommit` to currentHead
     - Prints message to stderr
     - Returns true (migration occurred)

4. **Move Shadow Branch**:
   - Creates new reference pointing to same commit as old shadow branch
   - Deletes old reference
   - Prints migration message to stderr
   - Updates `state.BaseCommit` to currentHead
   - Returns true

This is called from:
- `InitializeSession()` during UserPromptSubmit (before agent runs)
- `SaveChanges()` / `SaveTaskCheckpoint()` via `migrateAndPersistIfNeeded()`

### 7. **current-session.txt (ReadCurrentSession/WriteCurrentSession)**

**Current Usage**:
- `hooks.go:282` - `paths.WriteCurrentSession(input.SessionID)` in UserPromptSubmit (with TODO comment saying "keep this until we clean up gemini hooks")
- `debug.go:126` - `paths.ReadCurrentSession()` to display current session
- `hooks_geminicli_handlers.go:48` - `paths.ReadCurrentSession()` in SessionEnd handler for Gemini
- `hooks_git_cmd.go:67` - `paths.ReadCurrentSession()` in initHookLogging to get session ID for logging

**No references to `current-session.txt`** - the file is at `.entire/current_session` (no .txt extension)

**Key Points**:
- The file is only used by Gemini CLI integration and debug/logging
- Manual-commit strategy does NOT use this file for session management
- Manual-commit uses `.git/entire-sessions/<session-id>.json` files instead (via session.StateStore)
- The TODO comment in `hooks.go` suggests this is legacy and should be cleaned up

---

## Summary

The PostCommit flow is designed to be resilient and efficient:

1. **Checkpoint linking is opt-in**: Users can remove the trailer to skip condensation
2. **Checkpoint ID reuse**: Subsequent commits without new content reuse the last checkpoint ID
3. **Multi-session support**: Multiple concurrent sessions in the same worktree are condensed together
4. **Shadow branch cleanup**: Shadow branches are deleted after successful condensation to avoid clutter
5. **Attribution tracking**: Captures user vs agent contributions at each checkpoint
6. **Mid-session commit detection**: Uses live transcript analysis when shadow branch doesn't exist yet
7. **Migration support**: Automatically handles HEAD changes from pull/rebase operations

The `current-session.txt` mechanism is legacy (for Gemini CLI) and not used by the core manual-commit strategy flow.

}
```

### E. **`commitWithMetadata()` (auto-commit strategy)** (hooks_claudecode_handlers.go:~336-351)
**Concurrent with:** Stop hook
```go
sessionState, loadErr := strategy.LoadSessionState(sessionID)
// ... modifications ...
sessionState.CondensedTranscriptLines = totalLines
sessionState.CheckpointCount++
if updateErr := strategy.SaveSessionState(sessionState); updateErr != nil { ... }
```

### F. **`markSessionEnded()`** (hooks_claudecode_handlers.go:~717-732)
**Concurrent with:** SessionEnd hook
```go
state, err := strategy.LoadSessionState(sessionID)
now := time.Now()
state.EndedAt = &now
if err := strategy.SaveSessionState(state); err != nil { ... }
```

## 2. Syscall.Flock Availability

**Good news:** `syscall.Flock` IS available on macOS/Linux:

```
$ go doc syscall.Flock
package syscall // import "syscall"

func Flock(fd int, how int) (err error)
```

This is available on both target platforms (macOS and Linux). Constants like `syscall.LOCK_EX`, `syscall.LOCK_SH`, `syscall.LOCK_UN` are also available.

## 3. Existing Locking Patterns in the Codebase

I found **2 existing mutex patterns** but **NO existing file locking**:

### A. **`logging/logger.go`** - Protects logger state
```go
var (
    mu sync.RWMutex  // Protects logger, logFile, logBufWriter, currentSessionID
)
```
Used for in-memory logger initialization/cleanup.

### B. **`agent/registry.go`** - Protects agent registry
```go
var (
    registryMu sync.RWMutex  // Protects registry map
)
```
Used for agent factory registration/lookup.

**Key finding:** No existing file locking patterns exist. This will be a new pattern to introduce.

## 4. How `commitWithMetadata` Interacts with Session State

The Stop hook handler (`commitWithMetadata`) has a **race condition** with concurrent checkpoints:

```go
// hooks_claudecode_handlers.go:144-154
sessionState, loadErr := strategy.LoadSessionState(sessionID)
if loadErr != nil {
    fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Warning: failed to load session state: %v\n", loadErr)
}
if sessionState != nil {
    transcriptOffset = sessionState.CondensedTranscriptLines
    fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Session state found: parsing transcript from line %d\n", transcriptOffset)
}
```

**Race scenario:**
1. Stop hook loads state (line 147)
2. Concurrent checkpoint (PostTodo) modifies and saves state
3. Stop hook modifies the stale state and saves (lines 344-346), **overwriting** the concurrent checkpoint's modifications

## 5. Session State `Phase` Field

Looking at `session/state.go`, the `State` struct does **NOT** currently have a Phase field. Current fields relevant to tracking state:

```go
type State struct {
    SessionID       string
    BaseCommit      string
    EndedAt         *time.Time  // nil = active, non-nil = explicitly closed
    CheckpointCount int
    // ... other fields ...
}
```

**Options for phase tracking:**

### Option A: Add new `Phase` field
```go
type Phase string

const (
    PhaseActive    Phase = "active"     // Session is active
    PhaseCommitting Phase = "committing" // Stop hook is running
    PhaseClosed    Phase = "closed"     // Session explicitly closed
)

type State struct {
    Phase Phase `json:"phase,omitempty"`
    // ... existing fields ...
}
```

### Option B: Repurpose `EndedAt`
Current usage: `nil` = active, non-nil timestamp = closed via SessionEnd hook.

Could extend to distinguish Stop-in-progress, but this feels hacky since EndedAt has specific semantics.

**Recommendation:** Add new `Phase` field (Option A) for clarity and future extensibility.

## Summary of Race Conditions

The critical race is between:
- **Stop hook** (`commitWithMetadata`) - updates `CondensedTranscriptLines`, `CheckpointCount`
- **Concurrent checkpoints** (SaveChanges, SaveTaskCheckpoint) - update `CheckpointCount`, `FilesTouched`, `PromptAttributions`, `TokenUsage`

Without locking, both can:
1. Load the same state
2. Make independent modifications
3. Save, with last-write-wins overwriting the other's changes

This can cause lost checkpoint counts, missing files in `FilesTouched`, and incorrect attribution data.

> AGENT

Good point. Subagents fit into the model like this:

- **Subagents run during ACTIVE state** — they're spawned by the main agent within a turn
- **They write to the same shadow branch** via `SaveTaskCheckpoint()` (same session state, same `FilesTouched`)
- **Multiple subagents can run concurrently** — making the `flock` locking critical (multiple `SaveTaskCheckpoint` calls racing on the same state file)
- **Subagents CAN trigger git commits** — same mid-session commit problem as the main agent

So subagents don't need their own phase in the state machine — they operate within ACTIVE. But they're the strongest motivation for the `flock` locking, since concurrent subagents are a real (not theoretical) race condition.

Based on my exploration, I now have a clear understanding of the codebase structure. Let me create a detailed implementation plan:

## Implementation Plan: Session Phase State Machine & File Locking

This plan implements a session phase state machine and file locking to prevent premature condensation when agents commit mid-session.

### Phase 1: Foundation - File Locking Infrastructure (TDD)

**Objective**: Add file locking to StateStore to prevent race conditions

**Task 1.1: Add flock-based locking to StateStore**
- Add `LoadModifySave()` method to StateStore that wraps Load → modify → Save in flock
- Lock file: `<session-id>.json.lock` in same directory as state file
- Use `syscall.Flock` with `LOCK_EX` (exclusive lock, blocks other processes)
- Timeout: 10 seconds (configurable constant)
- Lock is released automatically when file descriptor closes (defer close)

Implementation pattern:
```go
// In session/state.go
func (s *StateStore) LoadModifySave(ctx context.Context, sessionID string, 
    modifyFn func(*State) error) error {
    // 1. Open lock file (create if missing)
    // 2. Acquire exclusive lock with timeout
    // 3. Load current state
    // 4. Call modifyFn to modify in-memory
    // 5. Save modified state
    // 6. Release lock (defer unlock + close)
}
```

**Task 1.2: Add unit tests for locking**
- Test concurrent LoadModifySave calls serialize correctly
- Test timeout when lock held too long
- Test lock released on error in modifyFn
- Test lock file created in correct location
- Test backwards compatibility (Load/Save still work without locking)

Files to create/modify:
- `cmd/entire/cli/session/state.go` - Add LoadModifySave method
- `cmd/entire/cli/session/state_test.go` - New test file for locking

**Task 1.3: Add helper for read-only loads**
- Keep existing `Load()` for read-only operations (no lock needed)
- Document when to use Load vs LoadModifySave

---

### Phase 2: Session Phase State Machine (TDD)

**Objective**: Add phase tracking to session state

**Task 2.1: Add Phase enum and field to State**
- Add Phase type: `type Phase string`
- Constants: PhaseActive, PhaseActiveCommitted, PhaseIdle, PhaseEnded
- Add Phase field to session.State struct
- Add PendingCheckpointID field (shared across all commits in session)
- Add LastInteractionTime field (for crash detection)
- Add helper methods: IsActive(), IsEnded(), ShouldCondense()

```go
// In session/state.go
type Phase string

const (
    PhaseActive           Phase = "active"
    PhaseActiveCommitted  Phase = "active_committed"
    PhaseIdle             Phase = "idle"
    PhaseEnded            Phase = "ended"
)

// Add to State struct:
Phase               Phase           `json:"phase,omitempty"`
PendingCheckpointID id.CheckpointID `json:"pending_checkpoint_id,omitempty"`
LastInteractionTime *time.Time      `json:"last_interaction_time,omitempty"`
```

**Task 2.2: Add state transition helpers**
- `TransitionTo(newPhase Phase) error` - validates legal transitions
- `CanCondense() bool` - returns true if session is eligible for condensation
- `IsTimedOut(ttl time.Duration) bool` - checks LastInteractionTime
- Add constants for TTL (30 minutes)

**Task 2.3: Add backward compatibility handling**
- Missing Phase field = PhaseIdle (existing sessions)
- Add migration logic in Load() to set default phase
- Document migration strategy in comments

**Task 2.4: Unit tests for phase transitions**
- Test valid transitions (ACTIVE → ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE, etc.)
- Test invalid transitions (ENDED → ACTIVE without clearing EndedAt)
- Test timeout detection
- Test backward compatibility (missing phase = idle)
- Test CanCondense() logic for each phase

Files to modify:
- `cmd/entire/cli/session/state.go` - Add Phase type, field, helpers
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_types.go` - Mirror Phase to SessionState
- `cmd/entire/cli/session/state_test.go` - Add phase transition tests

---

### Phase 3: Hook Integration - Phase Transitions (Build on Phases 1 & 2)

**Objective**: Update hooks to transition phases and use locking

**Task 3.1: Update InitializeSession to use locking and set ACTIVE**
- Replace direct Save with LoadModifySave
- Set Phase = PhaseActive on new prompt
- Clear EndedAt if resuming ended session
- Update LastInteractionTime
- If no PendingCheckpointID exists, generate one

In `manual_commit_hooks.go`:
```go
func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) InitializeSession(...) error {
    return s.stateStore.LoadModifySave(ctx, sessionID, func(state *State) error {
        // Existing initialization logic
        state.Phase = PhaseActive
        state.LastInteractionTime = timePtr(time.Now())
        if state.PendingCheckpointID.IsEmpty() {
            cpID, _ := id.Generate()
            state.PendingCheckpointID = cpID
        }
        // Clear EndedAt if resuming
        if state.EndedAt != nil {
            state.EndedAt = nil
        }
        return nil
    })
}
```

**Task 3.2: Update SaveChanges to use locking and update LastInteractionTime**
- Use LoadModifySave instead of load → modify → save pattern
- Update LastInteractionTime on every checkpoint
- Keep phase as-is (ACTIVE or ACTIVE_COMMITTED)

**Task 3.3: Update SaveTaskCheckpoint to use locking**
- Same pattern as SaveChanges
- Update LastInteractionTime

**Task 3.4: Update PrepareCommitMsg to read PendingCheckpointID**
- Use existing PendingCheckpointID from state (don't generate new one)
- Only generate new ID if none exists (backward compatibility)
- Use LoadModifySave if updating PendingCheckpointID

**Task 3.5: Unit tests for InitializeSession phase transitions**
- Test new session → ACTIVE
- Test IDLE → ACTIVE on new prompt
- Test ENDED → ACTIVE clears EndedAt
- Test PendingCheckpointID generation and reuse

Files to modify:
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go` - InitializeSession, PrepareCommitMsg
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_git.go` - SaveChanges, SaveTaskCheckpoint
- Add tests to existing test files

---

### Phase 4: Post-Commit Phase Detection & Shadow Branch Migration

**Objective**: Post-commit hook detects mid-session commits and updates phase

**Task 4.1: Extract shadow branch migration to reusable helper**
- Already exists in `manual_commit_migration.go` as `migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded`
- Make it public if needed for PostCommit
- Ensure it works without session state (can pass nil)

**Task 4.2: Update PostCommit to detect mid-session commits**
- Use LoadModifySave for atomic read-modify-write
- Check if any session has Phase = ACTIVE (agent still running)
- If ACTIVE: transition to ACTIVE_COMMITTED, migrate shadow branch, skip condensation
- If ACTIVE_COMMITTED: keep as-is, migrate shadow branch, skip condensation
- If IDLE or ENDED: proceed with condensation (existing behavior)

Logic:
```go
func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) PostCommit() error {
    sessions, _ := s.findSessionsForWorktree(worktreePath)
    
    for _, state := range sessions {
        s.stateStore.LoadModifySave(ctx, state.SessionID, func(s *State) error {
            if s.Phase == PhaseActive {
                // Agent committed mid-session
                s.Phase = PhaseActiveCommitted
                // Migrate shadow branch to new HEAD
                migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded(repo, s)
                // Update BaseCommit
                s.BaseCommit = newHead
                return nil // Skip condensation
            }
            if s.Phase == PhaseActiveCommitted {
                // Additional mid-session commit
                migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded(repo, s)
                s.BaseCommit = newHead
                return nil // Skip condensation
            }
            // IDLE or ENDED: condense
            return s.condenseSession(...)
        })
    }
}
```

**Task 4.3: Add logging for phase transitions**
- Log when ACTIVE → ACTIVE_COMMITTED
- Log when condensation skipped due to phase

**Task 4.4: Unit tests for PostCommit phase handling**
- Test ACTIVE session: transitions to ACTIVE_COMMITTED, no condensation
- Test ACTIVE_COMMITTED session: stays ACTIVE_COMMITTED, no condensation
- Test IDLE session: condensation happens
- Test shadow branch migration on mid-session commit

Files to modify:
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go` - PostCommit
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_migration.go` - Ensure public helper
- Add integration tests

---

### Phase 5: Stop Hook Condensation Trigger

**Objective**: commitWithMetadata detects ACTIVE_COMMITTED and triggers condensation

**Task 5.1: Add Stop-time phase transition in commitWithMetadata**
- Use LoadModifySave to atomically check and update phase
- If Phase = ACTIVE → IDLE (no mid-session commits)
- If Phase = ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE + trigger condensation
- If Phase = IDLE → IDLE (already condensed, e.g., user committed after Stop)

In `hooks_claudecode_handlers.go`:
```go
func commitWithMetadata() error {
    // ... existing Stop logic ...
    
    // Check if session had mid-session commits
    var needsCondensation bool
    s.stateStore.LoadModifySave(ctx, sessionID, func(state *State) error {
        if state.Phase == PhaseActiveCommitted {
            needsCondensation = true
        }
        // Transition to IDLE
        state.Phase = PhaseIdle
        return nil
    })
    
    // Call strategy SaveChanges (creates shadow branch checkpoint)
    strat.SaveChanges(...)
    
    // If had mid-session commits, condense now
    if needsCondensation {
        strat.CondenseSession(...)
    }
}
```

**Task 5.2: Update CondenseSession to handle Stop-time condensation**
- Already works for post-commit, should work for Stop-time too
- Ensure it doesn't double-condense (check if LastCheckpointID matches)
- Use PendingCheckpointID from session state

**Task 5.3: Unit tests for Stop-time condensation**
- Test ACTIVE session at Stop: no condensation (goes to IDLE)
- Test ACTIVE_COMMITTED at Stop: condensation triggered
- Test IDLE at Stop: no condensation (already done)

Files to modify:

- Add integration tests

### Phase 7: Shadow Branch Cleanup

**Objective**: Only delete shadow branches when ALL sessions are condensed

**Task 7.1: Update PostCommit shadow branch deletion logic**
- Track which sessions use which shadow branch
- Only delete branch when all sessions on it have been condensed
- Check Phase != ACTIVE and Phase != ACTIVE_COMMITTED for all sessions

Logic:
```go
// Before deleting shadow branch:
allSessionsCondensed := true
for _, state := range sessionsOnBranch {
    if state.Phase == PhaseActive || state.Phase == PhaseActiveCommitted {
        allSessionsCondensed = false
        break
    }
}
if allSessionsCondensed {
    deleteShadowBranch(shadowBranchName)
}
```

**Objective**: End-to-end testing of phase transitions

**Task 9.1: Integration test for mid-session commit**

- Verify: Shadow branch migrated, condensation happens at Stop

- Verify: Phase stays ACTIVE_COMMITTED, single condensation at Stop

- Verify: Shadow branches independent, phases tracked correctly

### Phase 10: Performance & Edge Cases

**Task 10.1: Optimize locking for read-heavy operations**
- Keep Load() unlocked for read-only queries (e.g., listing sessions)
- Only use LoadModifySave for mutations

**Task 10.2: Handle edge cases**
- Test behavior when .git/entire-sessions/ directory is deleted
- Test behavior when lock file is stale (process crashed while holding lock)
- Add lock file cleanup on uninstall

**Task 10.3: Add metrics/monitoring**
- Count lock contentions (if lock is already held)
- Count timeouts

---

## Testing Strategy

### Unit Tests (Run with each phase)
- Phase 1: Locking tests (concurrent access, timeouts)
- Phase 2: State machine tests (transitions, validation)
- Phase 3-7: Hook handler tests (mock git operations)

### Integration Tests (Run after Phase 9)
- Real git operations in temp repos
- Multi-process scenarios (concurrent hooks)
- Crash/recovery scenarios

### Backward Compatibility Tests
- Old session state files (missing Phase field)
- Mixed sessions (some with phase, some without)

---

## Rollout Plan

1. Deploy Phase 1-2 first (locking + state machine)
2. Monitor for locking timeouts in production
3. Deploy Phase 3-5 (hook integration)
4. Monitor phase transitions, condensation behavior
5. Deploy Phase 6-8 (cleanup + docs)
6. Collect feedback, iterate

---

## Risk Mitigation

1. **Lock timeout**: Set to 10s (generous for hook operations)
2. **Backward compat**: Missing phase = IDLE (safe default)
3. **Failed migrations**: Log errors but don't block hooks
4. **Stale locks**: OS releases lock when process exits (flock behavior)

---

### Critical Files for Implementation

The following files are most critical for implementing this plan:

- **cmd/entire/cli/session/state.go** - Core locking infrastructure (LoadModifySave), Phase enum and helpers. This is the foundation for all other changes.

- **cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go** - Phase transitions in hooks (InitializeSession, PrepareCommitMsg, PostCommit). This is where the state machine logic lives.

- **cmd/entire/cli/hooks_claudecode_handlers.go** - Stop hook condensation trigger (commitWithMetadata). Critical for ACTIVE_COMMITTED → condensation flow.

- **cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_types.go** - SessionState struct with Phase field. Needs to stay in sync with session.State.

- **cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_git.go** - SaveChanges and SaveTaskCheckpoint need locking for LastInteractionTime updates.

    30→	LastCheckpointID         id.CheckpointID `json:"last_checkpoint_id,omitempty"`         // Checkpoint ID from last condensation, reused for subsequent commits without new content

    32→	TranscriptPath           string          `json:"transcript_path,omitempty"`            // Path to the live transcript file (for mid-session commit detection)
    33→

    36→
