   128→- After SaveChanges:
   129→  - If was ACTIVE_COMMITTED → condense immediately, set phase=IDLE
   130→  - If was ACTIVE → set phase=IDLE (normal, condense on user commit)
   131→
   132→### Step 4: Phase-Aware PostCommit
   133→
   134→Update `PostCommit()` to check phase and defer condensation for active sessions.
   135→
   136→**Files:**
   137→- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go` — `PostCommit()`: for ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED sessions, skip condensation, update BaseCommit + migrate shadow branch, set phase to ACTIVE_COMMITTED
   138→- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_migration.go` — Ensure `migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded` works when called from PostCommit context
   139→
   140→**PostCommit logic per session:**
   141→```
   142→if phase == ACTIVE:
   143→    phase = ACTIVE_COMMITTED
   144→    migrate shadow branch to new HEAD
   145→    update BaseCommit
   146→    SKIP condensation
   147→
   148→if phase == ACTIVE_COMMITTED:
   149→    migrate shadow branch to new HEAD
   150→    update BaseCommit
   151→    SKIP condensation
   152→
   153→if phase == IDLE or ENDED or TIMED_OUT:
   154→    condense (existing behavior)
   155→```
   156→
   157→**Shadow branch deletion:**
   158→```
   159→canDelete = true
   160→for each session on this shadow branch:
   161→    if session.Phase == ACTIVE or ACTIVE_COMMITTED:
   162→        canDelete = false
   163→if canDelete:
   164→    delete shadow branch
   165→```
   166→
   167→### Step 5: Phase-Aware PrepareCommitMsg
   168→
   169→Update `PrepareCommitMsg()` to use `PendingCheckpointID` from session state.
   170→
   171→**Files:**
   172→- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go` — `PrepareCommitMsg()`: use existing PendingCheckpointID from state instead of generating new one each time
   173→
   174→**Logic:**
   175→- If any session is ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED with PendingCheckpointID: use it
   176→- If no PendingCheckpointID exists: generate one, store in state via LoadModifySave
   177→- All commits in a session share the same PendingCheckpointID
   178→
   179→### Step 6: Migrate Locking to All Call Sites
   180→
   181→Replace bare load+save patterns with `LoadModifySave` across all mutation sites.
   182→
   183→**Files:**
   184→- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_git.go` — `SaveChanges()`, `SaveTaskCheckpoint()`
   185→- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go` — `PostCommit()`, `InitializeSession()`
   186→- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_session.go` — Add `loadModifySaveSessionState()` wrapper
   187→
   188→**Approach:** The strategy layer calls `stateStore.LoadModifySave()` via a helper that handles the `session.State` ↔ `strategy.SessionState` conversion:
   189→
   190→```go
   191→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) loadModifySaveSessionState(
   192→    sessionID string, fn func(*SessionState) error,
   193→) error {
   194→    store, _ := s.getStateStore()
   195→    return store.LoadModifySave(ctx, sessionID, func(state *session.State) error {
   196→        stratState := sessionStateToStrategy(state)
   197→        if err := fn(stratState); err != nil { return err }
   198→        *state = *sessionStateFromStrategy(stratState)
   199→        return nil
   200→    })
   201→}
   202→```
   203→
   204→### Step 7: Remove current-session.txt
   205→
   206→Remove legacy single-session tracking.
   207→
   208→**Files to modify:**
   209→- `cmd/entire/cli/hooks.go:282` — Remove `paths.WriteCurrentSession()` call
   210→- `cmd/entire/cli/hooks_geminicli_handlers.go:48` — Remove `paths.ReadCurrentSession()` usage (get session ID from hook input instead)
   211→- `cmd/entire/cli/hooks_git_cmd.go:67` — `initHookLogging()`: get session ID from session state files instead of current_session file
   212→- `cmd/entire/cli/debug.go:126` — `debugShowCommand`: list active sessions from StateStore instead
   213→- `cmd/entire/cli/paths/paths.go` — Remove `ReadCurrentSession()`, `WriteCurrentSession()`, `CurrentSessionFile`
   214→- `cmd/entire/cli/paths/paths_test.go` — Remove related tests
   215→- `cmd/entire/cli/hook_registry_test.go` — Update tests that reference current_session
   216→- `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/hook_logging_test.go` — Update tests
   217→
   218→### Step 8: Integration Tests
   219→
   220→**Files:**
   221→- `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/phase_state_test.go` — New file
   222→
   223→**Test scenarios:**
   224→1. Mid-session commit: UserPromptSubmit → SaveChanges → git commit → verify ACTIVE_COMMITTED, no condensation
   225→2. Stop after mid-session commit: verify condensation happens at Stop
   226→3. Multiple mid-session commits: verify single condensation at Stop
   227→4. Concurrent sessions: one commits mid-session, verify independent phases
   228→5. Session resume: ENDED → ACTIVE clears EndedAt
   229→6. Shadow branch preserved: verify shadow branch NOT deleted while ACTIVE session exists
   230→7. `entire reset` with ACTIVE session: verify warning/refusal
   231→8. `git reset --hard` during ACTIVE_COMMITTED: verify post-commit migrates correctly
   232→
   233→### Step 9: CLAUDE.md Updates
   234→
   235→Update documentation to reflect:
   236→- Phase state machine description
   237→- New session state fields
   238→- Updated multi-session behavior
   239→- Locking behavior
   240→
   241→## Edge Cases: External Git Operations
   242→
   243→### `entire reset` while session is active
   244→
   245→`entire reset` deletes the shadow branch and all session state files for the current HEAD. If called while a session is ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED:
   246→- Agent's subsequent SaveChanges will fail to find state, falls back to `initializeSession()` (self-heals)
   247→- But all previous checkpoint data is lost
   248→
   249→**Action (Step 4):** Add guard in `Reset()` — if any session on the shadow branch has phase ACTIVE or ACTIVE_COMMITTED, warn and require `--force`.
   250→
   251→### `git stash` while IDLE
   252→
   253→- HEAD unchanged, no hooks fire
   254→- Shadow branch + session state untouched
   255→- `git stash pop` restores files
   256→- **Safe.** No changes needed.
   257→
   258→### `git reset --hard` (to HEAD) while IDLE
   259→
   260→- Cleans working directory, HEAD unchanged, no hooks fire
   261→- Agent's changes gone from working directory but **preserved on shadow branch**
   262→- User can `entire rewind` to restore
   263→- **Safe.** No changes needed.
   264→
   265→### `git reset --hard <older-commit>` while IDLE
   266→
   267→- HEAD moves backward, no hooks fire
   268→- Session state still has old BaseCommit
   269→- On next `UserPromptSubmit` → `migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded()` renames shadow branch
   270→- **Collision risk:** if the older commit already has a shadow branch, it gets overwritten
   271→- This is a pre-existing issue, not introduced by our changes
   272→- **Out of scope** for this PR, but noted for future hardening
   273→
   274→### `git rebase` while ACTIVE
   275→
   276→`prepare-commit-msg` already skips during sequence operations (rebase, cherry-pick, revert) — no trailer added. `post-commit` fires for each rebased commit: sees ACTIVE → transitions to ACTIVE_COMMITTED → migrates shadow branch. After rebase, shadow branch is at the new HEAD. `git rebase --abort` resets HEAD; next UserPromptSubmit migrates back. **Handled**, but many rapid migrations could be noisy (logging only, not user-visible).
   277→
   278→### `git reset --hard` while ACTIVE_COMMITTED
   279→
   280→- HEAD moves but agent is still running
   281→- Agent may do another commit → post-commit fires
   282→- Our new post-commit (Step 4) calls `migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded()` which handles the HEAD change
   283→- **Handled** by the migration in post-commit.
   284→
   285→## Coordination
   286→
   287→- **PR #165 (LastInteractionTime)**: This plan uses LastInteractionTime for crash/timeout detection. If PR #165 hasn't landed, we add the field ourselves. If it has, we reuse it.
   288→- **entire/sessions → entire/checkpoints/v1 rename**: Account for the branch rename in any new code.
   289→
   290→## Verification
   291→
   292→After implementation:
   293→```bash
   294→mise run fmt && mise run lint && mise run test:ci
   295→```
   296→
   297→Manual verification:
   298→1. Start a session, have agent commit mid-session, verify no condensation
   299→2. Stop the session, verify condensation happens
   300→3. Commit normally, verify trailer links to correct checkpoint
   301→4. Check `entire status` shows correct phase info
   302→5. Kill a session mid-work, verify timeout detection works on next commit
   303→

  "content": "# ENT-221 + ENT-194: Session Phase State Machine\n\n> **For Claude:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task.\n\n**Goal:** Introduce a session phase state machine so hooks can distinguish \"agent committed mid-session\" from \"user committed between sessions\", preventing premature condensation and data loss.\n\n**Architecture:** Add a Phase field to session state that tracks the session lifecycle (ACTIVE, ACTIVE_COMMITTED, IDLE, ENDED). Git hooks check the phase before deciding whether to condense. File locking prevents concurrent hook processes from corrupting state.\n\n**Tech Stack:** Go, go-git v5, syscall.Flock\n\n---\n\n## Context\n\nWhen an AI agent commits via Bash mid-session (between UserPromptSubmit and Stop hooks), `post-commit` fires and prematurely condenses session data, resets checkpoint count, and deletes the shadow branch. This causes:\n\n- **ENT-194**: Sequential agent commits produce empty/incomplete checkpoints because each `post-commit` condenses before the transcript is complete\n- **ENT-221**: No cross-hook state tracking means hooks can't distinguish \"agent committed mid-session\" from \"user committed between sessions\"\n\n## State Machine\n\n```\nUNINITIALIZED ──UserPromptSubmit──> ACTIVE\nACTIVE ──Stop──────────────────────> IDLE\nACTIVE ──git commit────────────────> ACTIVE_COMMITTED\nACTIVE_COMMITTED ──git commit──────> ACTIVE_COMMITTED\nACTIVE_COMMITTED ──Stop────────────> IDLE + CONDENSE (immediate)\nIDLE ──git commit──────────────────> IDLE + CONDENSE\nIDLE ──UserPromptSubmit────────────> ACTIVE\nIDLE ──SessionStop─────────────────> ENDED\nENDED ──git commit─────────────────> ENDED + CONDENSE (remaining data)\nENDED ──resume (UserPromptSubmit)──> ACTIVE (clear EndedAt)\nTIMED_OUT (virtual): LastInteractionTime > TTL, treated as ENDED\n```\n\n### Key Behaviors\n\n- **ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED**: `post-commit` defers condensation, only updates BaseCommit + migrates shadow branch\n- **ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE**: Stop triggers immediate condensation (agent's commits already exist on branch)\n- **Shadow branch deletion**: Only when ALL sessions on the branch are condensed\n- **All sessions included in condensation**: Even sessions with no file changes get transcript captured\n- **PendingCheckpointID**: Generated once, shared across all commits in a session\n\n### Timeout Detection\n\n**Tiered TTL:** Different contexts use different staleness thresholds:\n- **Post-commit hook**: 2-minute TTL. Agents interact frequently (tool calls every few seconds), so 2 min of silence strongly indicates the session is dead or interrupted.\n- **General cleanup** (UserPromptSubmit scanning other sessions): 30-minute TTL. More conservative since it's housekeeping.\n\n### Known Limitations\n\n**False-positive timeout on permission prompts:** If the agent is alive but blocked on a permission prompt for > 2 minutes and the user commits in another terminal, post-commit falsely treats the session as TIMED_OUT. Impact: premature condensation + shadow branch deletion. When the agent continues, SaveChanges creates a new shadow branch — session data is split across two condensations. Not data loss, just two entries instead of one.\n\n**Ctrl-C interruption:** When a user Ctrl-C's during agent work, Claude Code shows \"Interrupted - What should Claude do instead?\" but no hook fires. The session stays in ACTIVE.\n- If user types another prompt → UserPromptSubmit detects ACTIVE→ACTIVE transition → **catch-up checkpoint** captures the interrupted turn's data → continues normally\n- If user commits immediately (no new prompt first) → post-commit sees ACTIVE with recent LastInteractionTime → skips condensation. Data is safe on shadow branch, condenses later.\n- Potential future fix: Claude Code adds an \"interrupted\" hook (track upstream)\n\n---\n\n## Task 1: File Locking on StateStore\n\nAdd `flock`-based `LoadModifySave()` to `session.StateStore` for atomic read-modify-write.\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `cmd/entire/cli/session/state.go` — Add `LoadModifySave(ctx, sessionID, func(*State) error) error`\n- Modify: `cmd/entire/cli/session/state_test.go` — Concurrent access tests, error propagation tests\n\n**Design:**\n```go\nfunc (s *StateStore) LoadModifySave(ctx context.Context, sessionID string, fn func(*State) error) error {\n    lockPath := s.stateFilePath(sessionID) + \".lock\"\n    f, _ := os.OpenFile(lockPath, os.O_CREATE|os.O_RDWR, 0o600)\n    defer f.Close()\n    defer os.Remove(lockPath)\n    syscall.Flock(int(f.Fd()), syscall.LOCK_EX) // blocks until acquired\n    defer syscall.Flock(int(f.Fd()), syscall.LOCK_UN)\n\n    state, _ := s.Load(ctx, sessionID)\n    if state == nil { state = &State{SessionID: sessionID} }\n    if err := fn(state); err != nil { return err }\n    return s.Save(ctx, state)\n}\n```\n\n- Keep existing `Load()` unlocked for read-only operations\n- `LoadModifySave` is the only way to mutate state safely\n\n**Verification:** `mise run test` — new unit tests pass\n\n---\n\n## Task 2: Phase Field on Session State\n\nAdd `Phase` enum Written to `docs/plans/2026-02-06-session-phase-state-machine.md`. What else did you want to discuss?

> DEVELOPER

another couple of scenarios:
1. user chats with claude, creates some stuff - wants to bail. Closes claude, git restore, opens a new claude -> what happens now?
2. user chats with claude, creates some stuff - wants to bail. git restore, forgets they have claude open, opens a new claude -> what happens now?

> AGENT

Good scenarios. Let me trace through both:

### Scenario 1: Close Claude → git restore → new Claude

```
1. User works with Claude (turns happen: ACTIVE → IDLE → ACTIVE → IDLE)
   Shadow branch has checkpoints with file changes
2. User closes Claude → SessionStop → Phase = ENDED, EndedAt set
3. User does `git restore .` → no hooks fire, working directory reset to HEAD
4. User opens new Claude → new session ID
   → SessionStart (informational only)
   → UserPromptSubmit → new session initialized, Phase = ACTIVE
   
State:
  Old session: ENDED, CheckpointCount > 0, shadow branch has stale file data
  New session: ACTIVE, same BaseCommit (HEAD unchanged), shares shadow branch
```

When user eventually commits:
- `post-commit` sees old session (ENDED) → condenses it. The shadow branch checkpoint still has the reverted files. Transcript is accurate but file state is from before the restore.
- `post-commit` sees new session → condenses new work.

**Not broken**, but the old session's condensed data includes file references for work the user abandoned. The transcript is still valuable though.

One issue: `CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints` doesn't check `EndedAt` — it would show "1 other active conversation" for the ENDED session, which is misleading.

### Scenario 2: git restore with Claude still open → new Claude

```
1. User works with Claude (ACTIVE or IDLE)
2. User does `git restore .` in another terminal → no hooks fire
3. User opens new Claude → new session starts
   → Concurrent session warning shown (old session has checkpoints)
4. Old Claude still running
   → Next Stop: SaveChanges captures current working directory (post-restore)
     → checkpoint shows "no changes" or only new changes
   → Old checkpoints on shadow branch still have pre-restore file data
```

**Not broken** — but the old session's earlier checkpoints have stale file snapshots. The next checkpoint captures the post-restore state correctly.

### The deeper question

Both scenarios are about the user **abandoning work** without a mechanism to signal it. `git restore` doesn't fire hooks. There's no way to detect it happened.

Should we:
- Accept this as a known scenario (data is safe, just includes abandoned work)?
- Try to detect file state divergence on next checkpoint (diff working directory against last checkpoint)?
- Provide an explicit "abandon session" command?

> AGENT

Good observation. Today `entire reset` is blunt — it kills ALL sessions on the current HEAD's shadow branch. In the "abandon" scenario, the user wants to kill the *old* session but keep the *new* one alive. `entire reset` would nuke both since they share the same shadow branch.

**The difference:**

| | `entire reset` (current) | Targeted abandon |
|---|---|---|
| **Scope** | All sessions on current HEAD | Specific session by ID |
| **Shadow branch** | Deleted (affects all sessions) | Preserved (other sessions need it) |
| **Session state** | All cleared | Only target session cleared |
| **Use case** | "Start completely fresh" | "This particular session's work is abandoned" |

So yes, `entire reset` could be made more targeted:

```
entire reset                    # Current behavior: clear everything for this HEAD
entire reset --session <id>     # Clear one session, preserve shadow branch if others need it
entire reset --ended            # Clear all ENDED sessions (cleanup abandoned work)
```

Or even an interactive mode: `entire reset` shows a picker of sessions and lets you choose which to clear.

This feels like a separate concern from the state machine work though.

> AGENT

You're right to question this. Let me trace through what happens to the shadow branch and attribution.

### Shadow branch with abandoned session data

The shadow branch has interleaved commits from both sessions, each tagged with `Entire-Session: <session-id>` trailer:

```
shadow branch:
  commit 1: [Session A] - file1.txt modified, metadata for session A
  commit 2: [Session A] - file1.txt + file2.txt, metadata for session A  
  commit 3: [Session B] - file3.txt modified, metadata for session B  (after restore)
```

Each commit is a **full tree snapshot** (not a diff). `WriteTemporary` builds from the current working directory each time. So session B's commits don't include session A's reverted files — they capture the post-restore state.

### Condensation

`extractSessionData` filters by session ID trailer — it only reads commits matching the target session. So:
- Condensing session A: reads commits 1, 2 → gets the abandoned file data + transcript
- Condensing session B: reads commit 3 → gets only post-restore data

**No cross-contamination** between sessions during condensation.

### Attribution

Attribution is per-session:
- Session A's attribution: `baseTree → session A's last checkpoint tree` — shows agent added lines to file1.txt, file2.txt
- Session B's attribution: `baseTree → session B's last checkpoint tree` — shows only file3.txt changes

**Attribution is correct per-session**, but session A's condensed checkpoint will report "agent added X lines to file1.txt, file2.txt" — changes that the user subsequently reverted. This is historically accurate (the agent DID make those changes), but it inflates the aggregate stats if someone sums across checkpoints.

### The real question

Is it problematic to condense session A's data at all? The user abandoned that work. Two perspectives:

1. **Keep it**: The transcript is valuable (shows what was tried). The file state is historical. Stats may be slightly inflated.
2. **Drop it**: The user explicitly reverted. Condensing abandoned work adds noise. Better to either not condense ENDED sessions automatically, or require explicit `entire reset` to clean up first.

I think the practical answer is: **this is acceptable for now**. The abandoned session's data is still accurate as a record of what happened. The user chose to revert, but the conversation history is preserved. If we want to be more surgical later, a targeted reset command handles it.

Should I add this as a documented edge case in the plan and move on, or do you want to dig deeper into the attribution impact?

> AGENT

That's a clean set of rules. Let me formalize:

### Condensation Eligibility

| Phase | Has FilesTouched | Condense? |
|---|---|---|
| ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED | Yes | Yes (include) |
| ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED | No | Yes (context valuable, session ongoing) |
| IDLE | Yes | Yes (include) |
| IDLE | No | Yes (context valuable, session active) |
| ENDED | Yes | Yes (contributed to code) |
| ENDED | No | **No (discard)** |
| TIMED_OUT | Yes | Yes (contributed to code) |
| TIMED_OUT | No | **No (discard)** |

**Simplified rule:** Discard only when session is ENDED/TIMED_OUT **AND** `len(FilesTouched) == 0`.

And for the explicit removal: a targeted `entire reset --session <id>` that clears just that session's state. The file changes from that session remain in the working directory (user handles them manually via git).

Let me update the plan with these rules and the targeted reset.

  "plan": "# ENT-221 + ENT-194: Session Phase State Machine\n\n## Context\n\nWhen an AI agent commits via Bash mid-session (between UserPromptSubmit and Stop hooks), `post-commit` fires and prematurely condenses session data, resets checkpoint count, and deletes the shadow branch. This causes:\n\n- **ENT-194**: Sequential agent commits produce empty/incomplete checkpoints because each `post-commit` condenses before the transcript is complete\n- **ENT-221**: No cross-hook state tracking means hooks can't distinguish \"agent committed mid-session\" from \"user committed between sessions\"\n\nThe fix introduces a **session phase state machine** so hooks know the session's lifecycle stage, plus **file locking** to prevent concurrent hook processes from corrupting session state.\n\n## State Machine\n\n```\nUNINITIALIZED ──UserPromptSubmit──> ACTIVE\nACTIVE ──Stop──────────────────────> IDLE\nACTIVE ──git commit────────────────> ACTIVE_COMMITTED\nACTIVE_COMMITTED ──git commit──────> ACTIVE_COMMITTED\nACTIVE_COMMITTED ──Stop────────────> IDLE + CONDENSE (immediate)\nIDLE ──git commit──────────────────> IDLE + CONDENSE\nIDLE ──UserPromptSubmit────────────> ACTIVE\nIDLE ──SessionStop─────────────────> ENDED\nENDED ──git commit─────────────────> ENDED + CONDENSE (remaining data)\nENDED ──resume (UserPromptSubmit)──> ACTIVE (clear EndedAt)\nTIMED_OUT (virtual): LastInteractionTime > TTL, treated as ENDED\n```\n\n### Timeout Detection\n\n**Tiered TTL:** Different contexts use different staleness thresholds:\n- **Post-commit hook**: 2-minute TTL. Agents interact frequently (tool calls every few seconds), so 2 min of silence strongly indicates the session is dead or interrupted. Fast recovery for Ctrl-C/kill scenarios.\n- **General cleanup** (UserPromptSubmit scanning other sessions): 30-minute TTL. More conservative since it's housekeeping.\n\n### Known Limitation: False-Positive Timeout on Permission Prompts\n\nIf the agent is alive but blocked on a permission prompt for > 2 minutes and the user commits in another terminal, post-commit falsely treats the session as TIMED_OUT. Impact: premature condensation + shadow branch deletion. When the agent continues, SaveChanges creates a new shadow branch — session data is split across two condensations. Not data loss, just two entries instead of one. Acceptable given the rarity of this case.\n\n### Known Limitation: Ctrl-C Interruption\n\nWhen a user Ctrl-C's during agent work, Claude Code shows \"Interrupted - What should Claude do instead?\" — the agent is alive at the prompt, but no hook fires. The session stays in ACTIVE even though the agent is idle.\n\n- If user types another prompt → UserPromptSubmit detects ACTIVE→ACTIVE transition → **catch-up checkpoint** captures the interrupted turn's data → continues normally\n- If user commits immediately (no new prompt first) → post-commit sees ACTIVE with recent LastInteractionTime → skips condensation. **Data is safe** on shadow branch, just won't condense on that commit. Will condense on next commit after Stop fires, session ends, or TTL expires.\n- Potential future fix: Claude Code adds an \"interrupted\" hook (track upstream)\n\nKey behaviors:\n- **ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED**: `post-commit` defers condensation, only updates BaseCommit + migrates shadow branch\n- **ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE**: Stop triggers immediate condensation (agent's commits already exist on branch)\n- **Shadow branch deletion**: Only when ALL sessions on the branch are condensed or discarded\n- **PendingCheckpointID**: Generated once, shared across all commits in a session\n\n### Condensation Eligibility\n\nNot all sessions are condensed. Ended sessions that made no file contributions are discarded:\n\n| Phase | FilesTouched? | Action |\n|---|---|---|\n| ACTIVE / IDLE | Any | **Condense** (session ongoing, context valuable) |\n| ENDED / TIMED_OUT | Non-empty | **Condense** (contributed to code) |\n| ENDED / TIMED_OUT | Empty | **Discard** (no code contribution, noise) |\n\n**Simplified rule:** Discard only when `(ENDED or TIMED_OUT) AND len(FilesTouched) == 0`.\n\nAdditionally, users can explicitly remove a session from checkpointing via `entire reset --session <id>`. This clears the session state; any file changes remain in the working directory for the user to handle via git.\n\n## Implementation Steps\n\n### Step 1: File Locking on StateStore\n\nAdd `flock`-based `LoadModifySave()` to `session.StateStore` for atomic read-modify-write.\n\n**Files:**\n- `cmd/entire/cli/session/state.go` — Add `LoadModifySave(ctx, sessionID, func(*State) error) error`\n- `cmd/entire/cli/session/state_test.go` — Concurrent access tests, timeout tests, error propagation tests\n\n**Design:**\n```go\nfunc (s *StateStore) LoadModifySave(ctx context.Context, sessionID string, fn func(*State) error) error {\n    lockPath := s.stateFilePath(sessionID) + \".lock\"\n    f, _ := os.OpenFile(lockPath, os.O_CREATE|os.O_RDWR, 0o600)\n    defer f.Close()\n    defer os.Remove(lockPath)\n    syscall.Flock(int(f.Fd()), syscall.LOCK_EX) // blocks until acquired\n    defer syscall.Flock(int(f.Fd()), syscall.LOCK_UN)\n\n    state, _ := s.Load(ctx, sessionID)\n    if state == nil { state = &State{SessionID: sessionID} }\n    if err := fn(state); err != nil { return err }\n    return s.Save(ctx, state)\n}\n```\n\n- Keep existing `Load()` unlocked for read-only operations (listing, status)\n- `LoadModifySave` is the only way to mutate state safely\n\n### Step 2: Phase Field on Session State\n\nAdd `Phase` enum and `PendingCheckpointID` to both `session.State` and `strategy.SessionState`.\n\n**Files:**\n- `cmd/entire/cli/session/state.go` — Add `Phase` type + constants, `PendingCheckpointID` field, helper methods (`IsActive()`, `CanCondense()`, `IsTimedOut()`)\n- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_types.go` — Mirror `Phase` and `PendingCheckpointID` fields on `SessionState`\n- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit.go` — Update `sessionStateToStrategy()` and `sessionStateFromStrategy()` conversion functions\n- `cmd/entire/cli/session/state_test.go` — Phase transition tests, backward compat (missing phase = IDLE)\n\n**New types in `session/state.go`:**\n```go\ntype Phase string\nconst (\n    PhaseActive          Phase = \"active\"\n    PhaseActiveCommitted Phase = \"active_committed\"\n    PhaseIdle            Phase = \"idle\"\n    PhaseEnded           Phase = \"ended\"\n)\n```\n\n**New fields on `State`:**\n```go\nPhase               Phase           `json:\"phase,omitempty\"`             // empty = idle (backward compat)\nPendingCheckpointID id.CheckpointID `json:\"pending_checkpoint_id,omitempty\"`\n```\n\n**Backward compat:** Missing/empty Phase treated as `PhaseIdle`.\n\n### Step 3: Phase Transitions in Agent Hooks\n\nWire up `UserPromptSubmit`, `Stop`, and `SessionStop` to set phases using `LoadModifySave`.\n\n**Files:**\n- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go` — `InitializeSession()`: set Phase=ACTIVE, clear EndedAt on resume, update LastInteractionTime\n- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_git.go` — `SaveChanges()`: use `LoadModifySave`, update LastInteractionTime. Check if Phase==ACTIVE_COMMITTED and trigger condensation on Stop transition.\n- `cmd/entire/cli/hooks_claudecode_handlers.go` — `commitWithMetadata()` (Stop handler): after SaveChanges, check if phase was ACTIVE_COMMITTED and condense if so. Transition phase to IDLE.\n- `cmd/entire/cli/hooks_claudecode_handlers.go` — `markSessionEnded()`: use `LoadModifySave`, set Phase=ENDED\n\n**InitializeSession changes:**\n- IDLE/ENDED → ACTIVE: Set phase, clear EndedAt, generate PendingCheckpointID if missing\n- ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED → ACTIVE: Previous turn was interrupted (Ctrl-C, crash). **Catch-up checkpoint**: read live transcript from `CondensedTranscriptLines`/`TranscriptLinesAtStart`, create shadow branch checkpoint for the interrupted turn's data. Then reset to ACTIVE for new turn. This ensures interrupted turns don't lose transcript data.\n\n**Stop handler changes (commitWithMetadata):**\n- Load state, check phase BEFORE calling SaveChanges\n- After SaveChanges:\n  - If was ACTIVE_COMMITTED → condense immediately, set phase=IDLE\n  - If was ACTIVE → set phase=IDLE (normal, condense on user commit)\n\n### Step 4: Phase-Aware PostCommit\n\nUpdate `PostCommit()` to check phase and defer condensation for active sessions.\n\n**Files:**\n- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go` — `PostCommit()`: for ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED sessions, skip condensation, update BaseCommit + migrate shadow branch, set phase to ACTIVE_COMMITTED\n- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_migration.go` — Ensure `migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded` works when called from PostCommit context\n\n**PostCommit logic per session:**\n```\nif phase == ACTIVE:\n    phase = ACTIVE_COMMITTED\n    migrate shadow branch to new HEAD\n    update BaseCommit\n    SKIP condensation\n\nif phase == ACTIVE_COMMITTED:\n    migrate shadow branch to new HEAD\n    update BaseCommit\n    SKIP condensation\n\nif phase == IDLE:\n    condense (existing behavior)\n\nif phase == ENDED or TIMED_OUT:\n    if > AGENT

Right! When `SessionStart` fires for a session that already has a state file (resume via `claude -r` or new session with same ID), the session should be IDLE — not back to UNINITIALIZED. Otherwise between SessionStart and the first UserPromptSubmit, the session is "off the grid" and a commit would miss it.

Updated transition:

```
ENDED ──SessionStart──> IDLE     (re-entry, immediately condensable)
IDLE  ──SessionStart──> IDLE     (no-op, already condensable)
(none) ──SessionStart──> still UNINITIALIZED (no state file exists yet)
```

This means `SessionStart` needs to do a state file check:
- If state file exists with Phase = ENDED → set Phase = IDLE, clear EndedAt
- If state file exists with Phase = ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED → leave as-is (stale, will timeout or catch-up on next UserPromptSubmit)
- If state file exists with Phase = IDLE → no-op
- If no state file → UNINITIALIZED (current behavior, wait for UserPromptSubmit)

Currently `SessionStart` is purely informational (shows a message, writes current_session.txt). This adds a small state mutation to it.

  "plan": "# ENT-221 + ENT-194: Session Phase State Machine\n\n## Context\n\nWhen an AI agent commits via Bash mid-session (between UserPromptSubmit and Stop hooks), `post-commit` fires and prematurely condenses session data, resets checkpoint count, and deletes the shadow branch. This causes:\n\n- **ENT-194**: Sequential agent commits produce empty/incomplete checkpoints because each `post-commit` condenses before the transcript is complete\n- **ENT-221**: No cross-hook state tracking means hooks can't distinguish \"agent committed mid-session\" from \"user committed between sessions\"\n\nThe fix introduces a **session phase state machine** so hooks know the session's lifecycle stage, plus **file locking** to prevent concurrent hook processes from corrupting session state.\n\n## State Machine\n\n```\nUNINITIALIZED ──UserPromptSubmit──────> ACTIVE  (state file created)\nACTIVE ──Stop─────────────────────────> IDLE\nACTIVE ──git commit───────────────────> ACTIVE_COMMITTED\nACTIVE_COMMITTED ──git commit─────────> ACTIVE_COMMITTED\nACTIVE_COMMITTED ──Stop───────────────> IDLE + CONDENSE (immediate)\nIDLE ──git commit─────────────────────> IDLE + CONDENSE\nIDLE ──UserPromptSubmit───────────────> ACTIVE\nIDLE ──SessionStop────────────────────> ENDED\nENDED ──SessionStart (re-entry)───────> IDLE  (clear EndedAt, immediately condensable)\nENDED ──git commit────────────────────> ENDED + CONDENSE (if FilesTouched non-empty)\nIDLE/ENDED ──UserPromptSubmit─────────> ACTIVE (clear EndedAt if needed)\nTIMED_OUT (virtual): LastInteractionTime > TTL, treated as ENDED\n```\n\n**SessionStart re-entry:** When `SessionStart` fires and a state file exists:\n- Phase = ENDED → set Phase = IDLE, clear EndedAt (session re-entered, immediately condensable)\n- Phase = IDLE → no-op (already condensable)\n- Phase = ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED → leave as-is (stale session, will timeout or catch-up)\n- No state file → UNINITIALIZED (wait for UserPromptSubmit to create state)\n\n### Timeout Detection\n\n**Tiered TTL:** Different contexts use different staleness thresholds:\n- **Post-commit hook**: 2-minute TTL. Agents interact frequently (tool calls every few seconds), so 2 min of silence strongly indicates the session is dead or interrupted. Fast recovery for Ctrl-C/kill scenarios.\n- **General cleanup** (UserPromptSubmit scanning other sessions): 30-minute TTL. More conservative since it's housekeeping.\n\n### Known Limitation: False-Positive Timeout on Permission Prompts\n\nIf the agent is alive but blocked on a permission prompt for > 2 minutes and the user commits in another terminal, post-commit falsely treats the session as TIMED_OUT. Impact: premature condensation + shadow branch deletion. When the agent continues, SaveChanges creates a new shadow branch — session data is split across two condensations. Not data loss, just two entries instead of one. Acceptable given the rarity of this case.\n\n### Known Limitation: Ctrl-C Interruption\n\nWhen a user Ctrl-C's during agent work, Claude Code shows \"Interrupted - What should Claude do instead?\" — the agent is alive at the prompt, but no hook fires. The session stays in ACTIVE even though the agent is idle.\n\n- If user types another prompt → UserPromptSubmit detects ACTIVE→ACTIVE transition → **catch-up checkpoint** captures the interrupted turn's data → continues normally\n- If user commits immediately (no new prompt first) → post-commit sees ACTIVE with recent LastInteractionTime → skips condensation. **Data is safe** on shadow branch, just won't condense on that commit. Will condense on next commit after Stop fires, session ends, or TTL expires.\n- Potential future fix: Claude Code adds an \"interrupted\" hook (track upstream)\n\nKey behaviors:\n- **ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED**: `post-commit` defers condensation, only updates BaseCommit + migrates shadow branch\n- **ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE**: Stop triggers immediate condensation (agent's commits already exist on branch)\n- **Shadow branch deletion**: Only when ALL sessions on the branch are condensed or discarded\n- **PendingCheckpointID**: Generated once, shared across all commits in a session\n\n### Condensation Eligibility\n\nNot all sessions are condensed. Ended sessions that made no file contributions are discarded:\n\n| Phase | FilesTouched? | Action |\n|---|---|---|\n| ACTIVE / IDLE | Any | **Condense** (session ongoing, context valuable) |\n| ENDED / TIMED_OUT | Non-empty | **Condense** (contributed to code) |\n| ENDED / TIMED_OUT | Empty | **Discard** (no code contribution, noise) |\n\n**Simplified rule:** Discard only when `(ENDED or TIMED_OUT) AND len(FilesTouched) == 0`.\n\nAdditionally, users can explicitly remove a session from checkpointing via `entire reset --session <id>`. This clears the session state; any file changes remain in the working directory for the user to handle via git.\n\n## Implementation Steps\n\n### Step 1: File Locking on StateStore\n\nAdd `flock`-based `LoadModifySave()` to `session.StateStore` for atomic read-modify-write.\n\n**Files:**\n- `cmd/entire/cli/session/state.go` — Add `LoadModifySave(ctx, sessionID, func(*State) error) error`\n- `cmd/entire/cli/session/state_test.go` — Concurrent access tests, timeout tests, error propagation tests\n\n**Design:**\n```go\nfunc (s *StateStore) LoadModifySave(ctx context.Context, sessionID string, fn func(*State) error) error {\n    lockPath := s.stateFilePath(sessionID) + \".lock\"\n    f, _ := os.OpenFile(lockPath, os.O_CREATE|os.O_RDWR, 0o600)\n    defer f.Close()\n    defer os.Remove(lockPath)\n    syscall.Flock(int(f.Fd()), syscall.LOCK_EX) // blocks until acquired\n    defer syscall.Flock(int(f.Fd()), syscall.LOCK_UN)\n\n    state, _ := s.Load(ctx, sessionID)\n Remove related tests\n- `cmd/entire/cli/hook_registry_test.go` — Update tests that reference current_session\n- `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/hook_logging_test.go` — Update tests\n\n### Step 8: Targeted Session Reset\n\nAdd `--session` flag to `entire reset` for per-session cleanup.\n\n**Files:**\n- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_reset.go` — Add per-session reset logic\n- `cmd/entire/cli/commands/reset.go` (or wherever reset command lives) — Add `--session` flag\n\n**Logic:**\n```\nentire reset --session <id>:\n  1. Load session state by ID\n  2. Clear session state file\n  3. If no other sessions reference the shadow branch → delete shadow branch\n  4. If other sessions still need it → leave shadow branch\n  5. File changes remain in working directory (user handles via git)\n```\n\nAlso update the existing `entire reset` (no args) to check for ACTIVE sessions and warn/require `--force`.\n\n### Step 9: Integration Tests\n\n**Files:**\n- `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/phase_state_test.go` — New file\n\n**Test scenarios:**\n1. Mid-session commit: UserPromptSubmit → SaveChanges → git commit → verify ACTIVE_COMMITTED, no condensation\n2. Stop after mid-session commit: verify condensation happens at Stop\n3. Multiple mid-session commits: verify single condensation at Stop\n4. Concurrent sessions: one commits mid-session, verify independent phases\n5. Session resume: ENDED → ACTIVE clears EndedAt\n6. Shadow branch preserved: verify shadow branch NOT deleted while ACTIVE session exists\n7. `entire reset` with ACTIVE session: verify warning/refusal\n8. `git reset --hard` during ACTIVE_COMMITTED: verify post-commit migrates correctly\n\n### Step 9: CLAUDE.md Updates\n\nUpdate documentation to reflect:\n- Phase state machine description\n- New session state fields\n- Updated multi-session behavior\n- Locking behavior\n\n## Edge Cases: External Git Operations\n\n### `entire reset` while session is active\n\n`entire reset` deletes the shadow branch and all session state files for the current HEAD. If called while a session is ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED:\n- Agent's subsequent SaveChanges will fail to find state, falls back to `initializeSession()` (self-heals)\n- But all previous checkpoint data is lost\n\n**Action (Step 4):** Add guard in `Reset()` — if any session on the shadow branch has phase ACTIVE or ACTIVE_COMMITTED, warn and require `--force`.\n\n### `git stash` while IDLE\n\n- HEAD unchanged, no hooks fire\n- Shadow branch + session state untouched\n- `git stash pop` restores files\n- **Safe.** No changes needed.\n\n### `git reset --hard` (to HEAD) while IDLE\n\n- Cleans working directory, HEAD unchanged, no hooks fire\n- Agent's changes gone from working directory but **preserved on shadow branch**\n- User can `entire rewind` to restore\n- **Safe.** No changes needed.\n\n### `git reset --hard <older-commit>` while IDLE\n\n- HEAD moves backward, no hooks fire\n- Session state still has old BaseCommit\n- On next `UserPromptSubmit` → `migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded()` renames shadow branch\n- **Collision risk:** if the older commit already has a shadow branch, it gets overwritten\n- This is a pre-existing issue, not introduced by our changes\n- **Out of scope** for this PR, but noted for future hardening\n\n### `git rebase` while ACTIVE\n\n`prepare-commit-msg` already skips during sequence operations (rebase, cherry-pick, revert) — no trailer added. `post-commit` fires for each rebased commit: sees ACTIVE → transitions to ACTIVE_COMMITTED → migrates shadow branch. After rebase, shadow branch is at the new HEAD. `git rebase --abort` resets HEAD; next UserPromptSubmit migrates back. **Handled**, but many rapid migrations could be noisy (logging only, not user-visible).\n\n### `git reset --hard` while ACTIVE_COMMITTED\n\n- HEAD moves but agent is still running\n- Agent may do another commit → post-commit fires\n- Our new post-commit (Step 4) calls `migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded()` which handles the HEAD change\n- **Handled** by the migration in post-commit.\n\n### User abandons work (git restore) then starts new session\n\nUser works with Claude, creates files, then does `git restore .` and either closes Claude first (ENDED) or opens new Claude alongside (concurrent sessions).\n\n- Old session's `FilesTouched` still lists files (even though reverted)\n- If ENDED + FilesTouched non-empty → condensed with historical data (accurate record of what happened)\n- If user wants to fully discard → use `entire reset --session <id>`\n- New session starts fresh, shares shadow branch, no cross-contamination in condensation (filtered by session ID trailer)\n- Attribution is per-session, so abandoned session's stats don't affect active session\n\n## Coordination\n\n- **PR #165 (LastInteractionTime)**: This plan uses LastInteractionTime for crash/timeout detection. If PR #165 hasn't landed, we add the field ourselves. If it has, we reuse it.\n- **entire/sessions → entire/checkpoints/v1 rename**: Account for the branch rename in any new code.\n\n## Verification\n\nAfter implementation:\n```bash\nmise run fmt && mise run lint && mise run test:ci\n```\n\nManual verification:\n1. Start a session, have agent commit mid-session, verify no condensation\n2. Stop the session, verify condensation happens\n3. Commit normally, verify trailer links to correct checkpoint\n4. Check `entire status` shows correct phase info\n5. Kill a session mid-work, verify timeout detection works on next commit\n"
}

{

    {

    },

  "plan": "# ENT-221 + ENT-194: Session Phase State Machine\n\n## Context\n\nWhen an AI agent commits via Bash mid-session (between UserPromptSubmit and Stop hooks), `post-commit` fires and prematurely condenses session data, resets checkpoint count, and deletes the shadow branch. This causes:\n\n- **ENT-194**: Sequential agent commits produce empty/incomplete checkpoints because each `post-commit` condenses before the transcript is complete\n- **ENT-221**: No cross-hook state tracking means hooks can't distinguish \"agent committed mid-session\" from \"user committed between sessions\"\n\nThe fix introduces a **session phase state machine** so hooks know the session's lifecycle stage, plus **file locking** to prevent concurrent hook processes from corrupting session state.\n\n## State Machine\n\n```\nUNINITIALIZED ──UserPromptSubmit──────> ACTIVE  (state file created)\nACTIVE ──Stop─────────────────────────> IDLE\nACTIVE ──git commit───────────────────> ACTIVE_COMMITTED\nACTIVE_COMMITTED ──git commit─────────> ACTIVE_COMMITTED\nACTIVE_COMMITTED ──Stop───────────────> IDLE + CONDENSE (immediate)\nIDLE ──git commit─────────────────────> IDLE + CONDENSE\nIDLE ──UserPromptSubmit───────────────> ACTIVE\nIDLE ──SessionStop────────────────────> ENDED\nENDED ──SessionStart (re-entry)───────> IDLE  (clear EndedAt, immediately condensable)\nENDED ──git commit────────────────────> ENDED + CONDENSE (if FilesTouched non-empty)\nIDLE/ENDED ──UserPromptSubmit─────────> ACTIVE (clear EndedAt if needed)\nTIMED_OUT (virtual): LastInteractionTime > TTL, treated as ENDED\n```\n\n**SessionStart re-entry:** When `SessionStart` fires and a state file exists:\n- Phase = ENDED → set Phase = IDLE, clear EndedAt (session re-entered, immediately condensable)\n- Phase = IDLE → no-op (already condensable)\n- Phase = ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED → leave as-is (stale session, will timeout or catch-up)\n- No state file → UNINITIALIZED (wait for UserPromptSubmit to create state)\n\n### Timeout Detection\n\n**Tiered TTL:** Different contexts use different staleness thresholds:\n- **Post-commit hook**: 2-minute TTL. Agents interact frequently (tool calls every few seconds), so 2 min of silence strongly indicates the session is dead or interrupted. Fast recovery for Ctrl-C/kill scenarios.\n- **General cleanup** (UserPromptSubmit scanning other sessions): 30-minute TTL. More conservative since it's housekeeping.\n\n### Known Limitation: False-Positive Timeout on Permission Prompts\n\nIf the agent is alive but blocked on a permission prompt for > 2 minutes and the user commits in another terminal, post-commit falsely treats the session as TIMED_OUT. Impact: premature condensation + shadow branch deletion. When the agent continues, SaveChanges creates a new shadow branch — session data is split across two condensations. Not data loss, just two entries instead of one. Acceptable given the rarity of this case.\n\n### Known Limitation: Ctrl-C Interruption\n\nWhen a user Ctrl-C's during agent work, Claude Code shows \"Interrupted - What should Claude do instead?\" — the agent is alive at the prompt, but no hook fires. The session stays in ACTIVE even though the agent is idle.\n\n- If user types another prompt → UserPromptSubmit detects ACTIVE→ACTIVE transition → **catch-up checkpoint** captures the interrupted turn's data → continues normally\n- If user commits immediately (no new prompt first) → post-commit sees ACTIVE with recent LastInteractionTime → skips condensation. **Data is safe** on shadow branch, just won't condense on that commit. Will condense on next commit after Stop fires, session ends, or TTL expires.\n- Potential future fix: Claude Code adds an \"interrupted\" hook (track upstream)\n\nKey behaviors:\n- **ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED**: `post-commit` defers condensation, only updates BaseCommit + migrates shadow branch\n- **ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE**: Stop triggers immediate condensation (agent's commits already exist on branch)\n- **Shadow branch deletion**: Only when ALL sessions on the branch are condensed or discarded\n- **PendingCheckpointID**: Generated once, shared across all commits in a session\n\n### Condensation Eligibility\n\nNot all sessions are condensed. Ended sessions that made no file contributions are discarded:\n\n| Phase | FilesTouched? | Action |\n|---|---|---|\n| ACTIVE / IDLE | Any | **Condense** (session ongoing, context valuable) |\n| ENDED / TIMED_OUT | Non-empty | **Condense** (contributed to code) |\n| ENDED / TIMED_OUT | Empty | **Discard** (no code contribution, noise) |\n\n**Simplified rule:** Discard only when `(ENDED or TIMED_OUT) AND len(FilesTouched) == 0`.\n\nAdditionally, users can explicitly remove a session from checkpointing via `entire reset --session <id>`. This clears the session state; any file changes remain in the working directory for the user to handle via git.\n\n## PR Sequencing\n\n```\nPR 1: File Locking (foundational, no behavior change)\n  → Steps 1 + 6, with unit tests\n\nPR 2: Phase Field + State Machine Core (state tracking, no git hook changes)\n  → Steps 2 + 3, with unit + integration tests for phase transitions\n\nPR 3: Phase-Aware Git Hooks (the actual bug fix)\n  → Steps 4 + 5, with integration tests for mid-session commits\n\nPR 4: Cleanup + Polish\n  → Steps 7 + 8 + 9 + 10, with remaining tests + docs\n```\n\nEach PR ships with its own tests. PRs are independently reviewable and deployable in order.\n\n---\n\n## Implementation Steps\n\n### Step 1: File Locking on StateStore (PR 1)\n\nAdd `flock`-based `LoadModifySave()` to `session.StateStore` for atomic read-modify-write.\n\n**Files:**\n- `cmd/entire/cli/session/state.go` — Add `LoadModifySave(ctx, sessionID, func(*State) error) error`\n- `cmd/entire/cli/session/state_test.go` — Concurrent access tests, timeout tests, error propagation tests\n\n**Design:**\n```go\nfunc (s *StateStore) LoadModifySave(ctx context.Context, sessionID string, fn func(*State) error) error {\n    lockPath := s.stateFilePath(sessionID) + \".lock\"\n    f, _ := os.OpenFile(lockPath, os.O_CREATE|os.O_RDWR, 0o600)\n    defer f.Close()\n    defer os.Remove(lockPath)\n    syscall.Flock(int(f.Fd()), syscall.LOCK_EX) // blocks until acquired\n    defer syscall.Flock(int(f.Fd()), syscall.LOCK_UN)\n\n    state, _ := s.Load(ctx, sessionID)\n    if   "plan": "# ENT-221 + ENT-194: Session Phase State Machine\n\n## Context\n\nWhen an AI agent commits via Bash mid-session (between UserPromptSubmit and Stop hooks), `post-commit` fires and prematurely condenses session data, resets checkpoint count, and deletes the shadow branch. This causes:\n\n- **ENT-194**: Sequential agent commits produce empty/incomplete checkpoints because each `post-commit` condenses before the transcript is complete\n- **ENT-221**: No cross-hook state tracking means hooks can't distinguish \"agent committed mid-session\" from \"user committed between sessions\"\n\nThe fix introduces a **session phase state machine** so hooks know the session's lifecycle stage, plus **file locking** to prevent concurrent hook processes from corrupting session state.\n\n## State Machine\n\n```\nUNINITIALIZED ──UserPromptSubmit──────> ACTIVE  (state file created)\nACTIVE ──Stop─────────────────────────> IDLE\nACTIVE ──git commit───────────────────> ACTIVE_COMMITTED\nACTIVE_COMMITTED ──git commit─────────> ACTIVE_COMMITTED\nACTIVE_COMMITTED ──Stop───────────────> IDLE + CONDENSE (immediate)\nIDLE ──git commit─────────────────────> IDLE + CONDENSE\nIDLE ──UserPromptSubmit───────────────> ACTIVE\nIDLE ──SessionStop────────────────────> ENDED\nENDED ──SessionStart (re-entry)───────> IDLE  (clear EndedAt, immediately condensable)\nENDED ──git commit────────────────────> ENDED + CONDENSE (if FilesTouched non-empty)\nIDLE/ENDED ──UserPromptSubmit─────────> ACTIVE (clear EndedAt if needed)\nTIMED_OUT (virtual): LastInteractionTime > TTL, treated as ENDED\n```\n\n**SessionStart re-entry:** When `SessionStart` fires and a state file exists:\n- Phase = ENDED → set Phase = IDLE, clear EndedAt (session re-entered, immediately condensable)\n- Phase = IDLE → no-op (already condensable)\n- Phase = ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED → leave as-is (stale session, will timeout or catch-up)\n- No state file → UNINITIALIZED (wait for UserPromptSubmit to create state)\n\n### Timeout Detection\n\n**Tiered TTL:** Different contexts use different staleness thresholds:\n- **Post-commit hook**: 2-minute TTL. Agents interact frequently (tool calls every few seconds), so 2 min of silence strongly indicates the session is dead or interrupted. Fast recovery for Ctrl-C/kill scenarios.\n- **General cleanup** (UserPromptSubmit scanning other sessions): 30-minute TTL. More conservative since it's housekeeping.\n\n### Known Limitation: False-Positive Timeout on Permission Prompts\n\nIf the agent is alive but blocked on a permission prompt for > 2 minutes and the user commits in another terminal, post-commit falsely treats the session as TIMED_OUT. Impact: premature condensation + shadow branch deletion. When the agent continues, SaveChanges creates a new shadow branch — session data is split across two condensations. Not data loss, just two entries instead of one. Acceptable given the rarity of this case.\n\n### Known Limitation: Ctrl-C Interruption\n\nWhen a user Ctrl-C's during agent work, Claude Code shows \"Interrupted - What should Claude do instead?\" — the agent is alive at the prompt, but no hook fires. The session stays in ACTIVE even though the agent is idle.\n\n- If user types another prompt → UserPromptSubmit detects ACTIVE→ACTIVE transition → **catch-up checkpoint** captures the interrupted turn's data → continues normally\n- If user commits immediately (no new prompt first) → post-commit sees ACTIVE with recent LastInteractionTime → skips condensation. **Data is safe** on shadow branch, just won't condense on that commit. Will condense on next commit after Stop fires, session ends, or TTL expires.\n- Potential future fix: Claude Code adds an \"interrupted\" hook (track upstream)\n\nKey behaviors:\n- **ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED**: `post-commit` defers condensation, only updates BaseCommit + migrates shadow branch\n- **ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE**: Stop triggers immediate condensation (agent's commits already exist on branch)\n- **Shadow branch deletion**: Only when ALL sessions on the branch are condensed or discarded\n- **PendingCheckpointID**: Generated once, shared across all commits in a session\n\n### Condensation Eligibility\n\nNot all sessions are condensed. Ended sessions that made no file contributions are discarded:\n\n| Phase | FilesTouched? | Action |\n|---|---|---|\n| ACTIVE / IDLE | Any | **Condense** (session ongoing, context valuable) |\n| ENDED / TIMED_OUT | Non-empty | **Condense** (contributed to code) |\n| ENDED / TIMED_OUT | Empty | **Discard** (no code contribution, noise) |\n\n**Simplified rule:** Discard only when `(ENDED or TIMED_OUT) AND len(FilesTouched) == 0`.\n\nAdditionally, users can explicitly remove a session from checkpointing via `entire reset --session <id>`. This clears the session state; any file changes remain in the working directory for the user to handle via git.\n\n## PR Sequencing\n\n```\nPR 1: File Locking (foundational, no behavior change)\n  → Steps 1 + 6, with unit tests\n\nPR 2: Phase Field + State Machine Core (state tracking, no git hook changes)\n  → Steps 2 + 3, with unit + integration tests for phase transitions\n\nPR 3: Phase-Aware Git Hooks (the actual bug fix)\n  → Steps 4 + 5, with integration tests for mid-session commits\n\nPR 4: Cleanup + Polish\n  → Steps 7 + 8 + 9 + 10, with remaining tests + docs\n```\n\nEach PR ships with its own tests. ## Approved Plan:
# ENT-221 + ENT-194: Session Phase State Machine

## Context

When an AI agent commits via Bash mid-session (between UserPromptSubmit and Stop hooks), `post-commit` fires and prematurely condenses session data, resets checkpoint count, and deletes the shadow branch. This causes:

- **ENT-194**: Sequential agent commits produce empty/incomplete checkpoints because each `post-commit` condenses before the transcript is complete
- **ENT-221**: No cross-hook state tracking means hooks can't distinguish "agent committed mid-session" from "user committed between sessions"

The fix introduces a **session phase state machine** so hooks know the session's lifecycle stage, plus **file locking** to prevent concurrent hook processes from corrupting session state.

## State Machine

```
UNINITIALIZED ──UserPromptSubmit──────> ACTIVE  (state file created)
ACTIVE ──Stop─────────────────────────> IDLE
ACTIVE ──git commit───────────────────> ACTIVE_COMMITTED
ACTIVE_COMMITTED ──git commit─────────> ACTIVE_COMMITTED
ACTIVE_COMMITTED ──Stop───────────────> IDLE + CONDENSE (immediate)
IDLE ──git commit─────────────────────> IDLE + CONDENSE
IDLE ──UserPromptSubmit───────────────> ACTIVE
IDLE ──SessionStop────────────────────> ENDED
ENDED ──SessionStart (re-entry)───────> IDLE  (clear EndedAt, immediately condensable)
ENDED ──git commit────────────────────> ENDED + CONDENSE (if FilesTouched non-empty)
IDLE/ENDED ──UserPromptSubmit─────────> ACTIVE (clear EndedAt if needed)
TIMED_OUT (virtual): LastInteractionTime > TTL, treated as ENDED
```

**SessionStart re-entry:** When `SessionStart` fires and a state file exists:
- Phase = ENDED → set Phase = IDLE, clear EndedAt (session re-entered, immediately condensable)
- Phase = IDLE → no-op (already condensable)
- Phase = ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED → leave as-is (stale session, will timeout or catch-up)
- No state file → UNINITIALIZED (wait for UserPromptSubmit to create state)

### Timeout Detection

**Tiered TTL:** Different contexts use different staleness thresholds:
- **Post-commit hook**: 2-minute TTL. Agents interact frequently (tool calls every few seconds), so 2 min of silence strongly indicates the session is dead or interrupted. Fast recovery for Ctrl-C/kill scenarios.
- **General cleanup** (UserPromptSubmit scanning other sessions): 30-minute TTL. More conservative since it's housekeeping.

### Known Limitation: False-Positive Timeout on Permission Prompts

If the agent is alive but blocked on a permission prompt for > 2 minutes and the user commits in another terminal, post-commit falsely treats the session as TIMED_OUT. Impact: premature condensation + shadow branch deletion. When the agent continues, SaveChanges creates a new shadow branch — session data is split across two condensations. Not data loss, just two entries instead of one. Acceptable given the rarity of this case.

### Known Limitation: Ctrl-C Interruption

When a user Ctrl-C's during agent work, Claude Code shows "Interrupted - What should Claude do instead?" — the agent is alive at the prompt, but no hook fires. The session stays in ACTIVE even though the agent is idle.

- If user types another prompt → UserPromptSubmit detects ACTIVE→ACTIVE transition → **catch-up checkpoint** captures the interrupted turn's data → continues normally
- If user commits immediately (no new prompt first) → post-commit sees ACTIVE with recent LastInteractionTime → skips condensation. **Data is safe** on shadow branch, just won't condense on that commit. Will condense on next commit after Stop fires, session ends, or TTL expires.
- Potential future fix: Claude Code adds an "interrupted" hook (track upstream)

Key behaviors:
- **ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED**: `post-commit` defers condensation, only updates BaseCommit + migrates shadow branch
- **ACTIVE_COMMITTED → IDLE**: Stop triggers immediate condensation (agent's commits already exist on branch)
- **Shadow branch deletion**: Only when ALL sessions on the branch are condensed or discarded
- **PendingCheckpointID**: Generated once, shared across all commits in a session

### Condensation Eligibility

Not all sessions are condensed. Ended sessions that made no file contributions are discarded:

| Phase | FilesTouched? | Action |
|---|---|---|
| ACTIVE / IDLE | Any | **Condense** (session ongoing, context valuable) |
| ENDED / TIMED_OUT | Non-empty | **Condense** (contributed to code) |
| ENDED / TIMED_OUT | Empty | **Discard** (no code contribution, noise) |

**Simplified rule:** Discard only when `(ENDED or TIMED_OUT) AND len(FilesTouched) == 0`.

Additionally, users can explicitly remove a session from checkpointing via `entire reset --session <id>`. This clears the session state; any file changes remain in the working directory for the user to handle via git.

## PR Sequencing

```
PR 1: File Locking (foundational, no behavior change)
  → Steps 1 + 6, with unit tests

PR 2: Phase Field + State Machine Core (state tracking, no git hook changes)
  → Steps 2 + 3, with unit + integration tests for phase transitions

PR 3: Phase-Aware Git Hooks (the actual bug fix)
  → Steps 4 + 5, with integration tests for mid-session commits

PR 4: Cleanup + Polish
  → Steps 7 + 8 + 9 + 10, with remaining tests + docs
```

Each PR ships with its own tests. PRs are independently reviewable and deployable in order.

---

## Implementation Steps

### Step 1: File Locking on StateStore (PR 1)

Add `flock`-based `LoadModifySave()` to `session.StateStore` for atomic read-modify-write.

**Files:**
- `cmd/entire/cli/session/state.go` — Add `LoadModifySave(ctx, sessionID, func(*State) error) error`
- `cmd/entire/cli/session/state_test.go` — Concurrent access tests, timeout tests, error propagation tests

**Design:**
```go
func (s *StateStore) LoadModifySave(ctx context.Context, sessionID string, fn func(*State) error) error {
    lockPath := s.stateFilePath(sessionID) + ".lock"
    f, _ := os.OpenFile(lockPath, os.O_CREATE|os.O_RDWR, 0o600)
    defer f.Close()
    defer os.Remove(lockPath)
    syscall.Flock(int(f.Fd()), syscall.LOCK_EX) // blocks until acquired
    defer syscall.Flock(int(f.Fd()), syscall.LOCK_UN)

    state, _ := s.Load(ctx, sessionID)
    if state == nil { state = &State{SessionID: sessionID} }
    if err := fn(state); err != nil { return err }
    return s.Save(ctx, state)
}
```

- Keep existing `Load()` unlocked for read-only operations (listing, status)
- `LoadModifySave` is the only way to mutate state safely

### Step 2: Phase Field on Session State (PR 2)

Add `Phase` enum and `PendingCheckpointID` to both `session.State` and `strategy.SessionState`.

**Files:**
- `cmd/entire/cli/session/state.go` — Add `Phase` type + constants, `PendingCheckpointID` field, helper methods (`IsActive()`, `CanCondense()`, `IsTimedOut()`)
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_types.go` — Mirror `Phase` and `PendingCheckpointID` fields on `SessionState`
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit.go` — Update `sessionStateToStrategy()` and `sessionStateFromStrategy()` conversion functions
- `cmd/entire/cli/session/state_test.go` — Phase transition tests, backward compat (missing phase = IDLE)

**New types in `session/state.go`:**
```go
type Phase string
const (
    PhaseActive          Phase = "active"
    PhaseActiveCommitted Phase = "active_committed"
    PhaseIdle            Phase = "idle"
    PhaseEnded           Phase = "ended"
)
```

**New fields on `State`:**
```go
Phase               Phase           `json:"phase,omitempty"`             // empty = idle (backward compat)
PendingCheckpointID id.CheckpointID `json:"pending_checkpoint_id,omitempty"`
```

**Backward compat:** Missing/empty Phase treated as `PhaseIdle`.

### Step 3: Phase Transitions in Agent Hooks (PR 2)

Wire up `SessionStart`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `Stop`, and `SessionStop` to set phases using `LoadModifySave`.

**Files:**
- `cmd/entire/cli/hooks.go` — `handleSessionStartCommon()`: check for existing state file, transition ENDED → IDLE on re-entry
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go` — `InitializeSession()`: set Phase=ACTIVE, clear EndedAt on resume, update LastInteractionTime
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_git.go` — `SaveChanges()`: use `LoadModifySave`, update LastInteractionTime. Check if Phase==ACTIVE_COMMITTED and trigger condensation on Stop transition.
- `cmd/entire/cli/hooks_claudecode_handlers.go` — `commitWithMetadata()` (Stop handler): after SaveChanges, check if phase was ACTIVE_COMMITTED and condense if so. Transition phase to IDLE.
- `cmd/entire/cli/hooks_claudecode_handlers.go` — `markSessionEnded()`: use `LoadModifySave`, set Phase=ENDED

**InitializeSession changes:**
- IDLE/ENDED → ACTIVE: Set phase, clear EndedAt, generate PendingCheckpointID if missing
- ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED → ACTIVE: Previous turn was interrupted (Ctrl-C, crash). **Catch-up checkpoint**: read live transcript from `CondensedTranscriptLines`/`TranscriptLinesAtStart`, create shadow branch checkpoint for the interrupted turn's data. Then reset to ACTIVE for new turn. This ensures interrupted turns don't lose transcript data.

**Stop handler changes (commitWithMetadata):**
- Load state, check phase BEFORE calling SaveChanges
- After SaveChanges:
  - If was ACTIVE_COMMITTED → condense immediately, set phase=IDLE
  - If was ACTIVE → set phase=IDLE (normal, condense on user commit)

### Step 4: Phase-Aware PostCommit (PR 3)

Update `PostCommit()` to check phase and defer condensation for active sessions.

**Files:**
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go` — `PostCommit()`: for ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED sessions, skip condensation, update BaseCommit + migrate shadow branch, set phase to ACTIVE_COMMITTED
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_migration.go` — Ensure `migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded` works when called from PostCommit context

**PostCommit logic per session:**
```
if phase == ACTIVE:
    phase = ACTIVE_COMMITTED
    migrate shadow branch to new HEAD
    update BaseCommit
    SKIP condensation

if phase == ACTIVE_COMMITTED:
    migrate shadow branch to new HEAD
    update BaseCommit
    SKIP condensation

if phase == IDLE:
    condense (existing behavior)

if phase == ENDED or TIMED_OUT:
    if len(FilesTouched) > 0 → condense (session contributed code)
    if len(FilesTouched) == 0 → discard (no code contribution, clear state)
```

**Shadow branch deletion:**
```
canDelete = true
for each session on this shadow branch:
    if session.Phase == ACTIVE or ACTIVE_COMMITTED:
        canDelete = false
if canDelete:
    delete shadow branch
```

### Step 5: Phase-Aware PrepareCommitMsg (PR 3)

Update `PrepareCommitMsg()` to use `PendingCheckpointID` from session state.

**Files:**
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go` — `PrepareCommitMsg()`: use existing PendingCheckpointID from state instead of generating new one each time

**Logic:**
- If any session is ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED with PendingCheckpointID: use it
- If no PendingCheckpointID exists: generate one, store in state via LoadModifySave
- All commits in a session share the same PendingCheckpointID

### Step 6: Migrate Locking to All Call Sites (PR 1)

Replace bare load+save patterns with `LoadModifySave` across all mutation sites.

**Files:**
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_git.go` — `SaveChanges()`, `SaveTaskCheckpoint()`
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go` — `PostCommit()`, `InitializeSession()`
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_session.go` — Add `loadModifySaveSessionState()` wrapper

**Approach:** The strategy layer calls `stateStore.LoadModifySave()` via a helper that handles the `session.State` ↔ `strategy.SessionState` conversion:

```go
func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) loadModifySaveSessionState(
    sessionID string, fn func(*SessionState) error,
) error {
    store, _ := s.getStateStore()
    return store.LoadModifySave(ctx, sessionID, func(state *session.State) error {
        stratState := sessionStateToStrategy(state)
        if err := fn(stratState); err != nil { return err }
        *state = *sessionStateFromStrategy(stratState)
        return nil
    })
}
```

### Step 7: Remove current-session.txt (PR 4)

Remove legacy single-session tracking.

**Files to modify:**
- `cmd/entire/cli/hooks.go:282` — Remove `paths.WriteCurrentSession()` call
- `cmd/entire/cli/hooks_geminicli_handlers.go:48` — Remove `paths.ReadCurrentSession()` usage (get session ID from hook input instead)
- `cmd/entire/cli/hooks_git_cmd.go:67` — `initHookLogging()`: get session ID from session state files instead of current_session file
- `cmd/entire/cli/debug.go:126` — `debugShowCommand`: list active sessions from StateStore instead
- `cmd/entire/cli/paths/paths.go` — Remove `ReadCurrentSession()`, `WriteCurrentSession()`, `CurrentSessionFile`
- `cmd/entire/cli/paths/paths_test.go` — Remove related tests
- `cmd/entire/cli/hook_registry_test.go` — Update tests that reference current_session
- `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/hook_logging_test.go` — Update tests

### Step 8: Targeted Session Reset (PR 4)

Add `--session` flag to `entire reset` for per-session cleanup.

**Files:**
- `cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_reset.go` — Add per-session reset logic
- `cmd/entire/cli/commands/reset.go` (or wherever reset command lives) — Add `--session` flag

**Logic:**
```
entire reset --session <id>:
  1. Load session state by ID
  2. Clear session state file
  3. If no other sessions reference the shadow branch → delete shadow branch
  4. If other sessions still need it → leave shadow branch
  5. File changes remain in working directory (user handles via git)
```

Also update the existing `entire reset` (no args) to check for ACTIVE sessions and warn/require `--force`.

### Step 9: Integration Tests (PR 2 + PR 3)

**Files:**
- `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/phase_state_test.go` — New file

**Test scenarios:**
1. Mid-session commit: UserPromptSubmit → SaveChanges → git commit → verify ACTIVE_COMMITTED, no condensation
2. Stop after mid-session commit: verify condensation happens at Stop
3. Multiple mid-session commits: verify single condensation at Stop
4. Concurrent sessions: one commits mid-session, verify independent phases
5. Session resume: ENDED → ACTIVE clears EndedAt
6. Shadow branch preserved: verify shadow branch NOT deleted while ACTIVE session exists
7. `entire reset` with ACTIVE session: verify warning/refusal
8. `git reset --hard` during ACTIVE_COMMITTED: verify post-commit migrates correctly

### Step 10: CLAUDE.md Updates (PR 4)

Update documentation to reflect:
- Phase state machine description
- New session state fields
- Updated multi-session behavior
- Locking behavior

## Edge Cases: External Git Operations

### `entire reset` while session is active

`entire reset` deletes the shadow branch and all session state files for the current HEAD. If called while a session is ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED:
- Agent's subsequent SaveChanges will fail to find state, falls back to `initializeSession()` (self-heals)
- But all previous checkpoint data is lost

**Action (Step 4):** Add guard in `Reset()` — if any session on the shadow branch has phase ACTIVE or ACTIVE_COMMITTED, warn and require `--force`.

### `git stash` while IDLE

- HEAD unchanged, no hooks fire
- Shadow branch + session state untouched
- `git stash pop` restores files
- **Safe.** No changes needed.

### `git reset --hard` (to HEAD) while IDLE

- Cleans working directory, HEAD unchanged, no hooks fire
- Agent's changes gone from working directory but **preserved on shadow branch**
- User can `entire rewind` to restore
- **Safe.** No changes needed.

### `git reset --hard <older-commit>` while IDLE

- HEAD moves backward, no hooks fire
- Session state still has old BaseCommit
- On next `UserPromptSubmit` → `migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded()` renames shadow branch
- **Collision risk:** if the older commit already has a shadow branch, it gets overwritten
- This is a pre-existing issue, not introduced by our changes
- **Out of scope** for this PR, but noted for future hardening

### `git rebase` while ACTIVE

`prepare-commit-msg` already skips during sequence operations (rebase, cherry-pick, revert) — no trailer added. `post-commit` fires for each rebased commit: sees ACTIVE → transitions to ACTIVE_COMMITTED → migrates shadow branch. After rebase, shadow branch is at the new HEAD. `git rebase --abort` resets HEAD; next UserPromptSubmit migrates back. **Handled**, but many rapid migrations could be noisy (logging only, not user-visible).

### `git reset --hard` while ACTIVE_COMMITTED

- HEAD moves but agent is still running
- Agent may do another commit → post-commit fires
- Our new post-commit (Step 4) calls `migrateShadowBranchIfNeeded()` which handles the HEAD change
- **Handled** by the migration in post-commit.

### User abandons work (git restore) then starts new session

User works with Claude, creates files, then does `git restore .` and either closes Claude first (ENDED) or opens new Claude alongside (concurrent sessions).

- Old session's `FilesTouched` still lists files (even though reverted)
- If ENDED + FilesTouched non-empty → condensed with historical data (accurate record of what happened)
- If user wants to fully discard → use `entire reset --session <id>`
- New session starts fresh, shares shadow branch, no cross-contamination in condensation (filtered by session ID trailer)
- Attribution is per-session, so abandoned session's stats don't affect active session

## Coordination

- **PR #165 (LastInteractionTime)**: This plan uses LastInteractionTime for crash/timeout detection. If PR #165 hasn't landed, we add the field ourselves. If it has, we reuse it.
- **entire/sessions → entire/checkpoints/v1 rename**: Account for the branch rename in any new code.

## Verification

After implementation:
```bash
mise run fmt && mise run lint && mise run test:ci
```

Manual verification:
1. Start a session, have agent commit mid-session, verify no condensation
2. Stop the session, verify condensation happens
3. Commit normally, verify trailer links to correct checkpoint
4. Check `entire status` shows correct phase info
5. Kill a session mid-work, verify timeout detection works on next commit
